# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_89951925.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 28, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave., and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and 
Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Johnson and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #21 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon 
request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item 
#26(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 14, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for renewal applications for existing City liquor 
licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite 
distillery, special malt beverage and winery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  There 
being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed 
the public hearing and referred the applications to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership and location of retail 
liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Dillon Companies, LLC, a Kansas 
limited liability company d/b/a King Soopers, 3702 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(license currently held by Avanti, Inc., d/b/a Dell Range Liquor, 3806 Dell Range Blvd. #4, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Kevin Georges expressed concern regarding the proposed transfer of the 
license to a major corporation and recent prior retail liquor license transfers that have went to large 
corporations.  Mr. Georges expressed the need of smaller local businesses having the opportunity 
to obtain retail liquor licenses. Matt Kim-Miller, attorney with Holland & Hart, LLP (applicant’s 
representative), provided information on the request noting the transfer involves a long-standing 
package liquor store location which will be expanded, noting he believes the location is appropriate 
for continued package liquor services for the community.  Upon inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones 
explained the license was originally issued several years ago and the request is for a transfer of 
location and ownership and that the additional retail liquor license awarded to the City based on 
the 2020 census has not been awarded yet.  Comment was made regarding the lack of liquor 
licensing options in the state.   Upon further inquiry, Ms. Jones advised it was her understanding 

that King Soopers will expand the existing package liquor location and open the access from the 
King Soopers store into the current liquor store location.  Mr. Kim-Miller confirmed the plan is to 
expand the existing liquor store space into the prior Hallmark location and there will be a doorway 
into King Soopers where point of sale will occur. Upon inquiry, Mr. Kim-Miller advised the 
proposal is to have doors between the grocery and liquor spaces.  There being no further comments 
or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and 
referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent 
to enter into a contract with Comea Shelter to grant a subaward from Coronavirus State and Local 
Fiscal Recovery Funds to be used to increase available emergency housing and temporary shelter 
for Cheyenne’s homeless population.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 13.04 – Public Utilities Generally; 
Chapter 13.08 – Water Service System; Chapter 13.12 – Sewer System; Chapter 13.16 – Storm 
Sewers; and Chapter 13.20 – Wastewater Treatment & Disposal; of Title 13 – Public Services, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. White moved to amend by 
substitute dated February 17, 2022, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Brad Brooks, 
Director of Board of Public Utilities, provided information on the proposed substitute noting the 
language changes in sections 13.04.050 and 13.04.120.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4416) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts 
and Uses, amending the Permissibility of Various Uses.  Brenda Birkle and Dan Dorsch, 
Affordable Housing Task Force, spoke in support of the ordinance noting it will contribute to 
affordable housing in the community.  Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and Development, 
provided information on a request to include a change for “Entertainment, Amusement, 
Recreation-Other” from a Conditional Use to a Permitted use in the CBD zoning district noting 
the change would remove the requirement for these types of businesses in the downtown area to 
go before the Board of Adjustment for approval resulting in being able to go into business sooner.  
Mr. Bloom advised staff has reviewed the change request and believes it is appropriate for the 
downtown CBD zone.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend Attachment “A” to change 
“Entertainment, Amusement, Recreation-Other” from a Conditional Use to a Permitted Use in the 
CBD zoning district, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry related to a scenario where an 
outdoor shooting range business could potentially open a business in the downtown area without 
notification, Mr. Bloom and Mr. Johnson provided information on existing ordinances that would 
prevent that type of business in the downtown area.  Motion to amend by Attachment A carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  
 

ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint.  Mr. Cook moved 
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute 
dated February 24, 2022, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and 
Development, provided information on the substitute noting the acreage was changed from 100 to 
80 for when developments can submit a common landscape plan.  Motion to amend by substitute 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use to AG Agricultural for 
land located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to HI Heavy 
Industrial for land located north of Campstool Road, west of Progress Circle.  Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Cook confirmed the zone change request is to allow for outdoor storage.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 9.08.120, Malicious Harassment 
Based on Race, Color, Religion, Ancestry, Sex, Sexual Orientation, Gender Identity and 
Expression, National Origin, or Disability, of Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and 
Decency, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  
Mayor Collins announced there would be a 3-minute time limit for speakers and that comments 
and discussion should remain cordial. Speaking in opposition of the ordinance were: Robin 
Goodspeed, Eric Crosby, Jay Meyer, Andrew Dimit, Steven Edgar Olson, Nick Dawson, Alicia 
Green, Bob Wilson, Steve Milliad, Matt Freeman, Angelina Freeman, Sandra Cane and Mark   
Moss.  Topics discussed included: existing statutes, ordinances and policies that address bullying, 
harassment and violence; potential of ordinance being harmful towards individuals with opposing 
beliefs; affect the ordinance may have on religious groups; national events relating to similar 
ordinances in other states; confusion and challenges relating to the ordinance; biblical beliefs; how 
reporting offences to the Police Department will be handled; concern that non-supporters of the 
ordinance don’t support the protected groups outlined in the ordinance; belief that Cheyenne is not 
in need of the ordinance; suggestion to approach harassment concerns in other ways and request 
to allow more community input related to the ordinance.  Speaking in support of the ordinance 
were: Amber Leyba and Dale Steenbergen, Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, Troy 
Rumpf, Stuart Campbell and Keil Bergeron.  Topics discussed included: ordinance importance 
relating to economic development; witnessed threats and slurs; importance of ordinance as a 
statement of support for the protected groups and the need for the ordinance.  Additional comments 

were made that some companies have concerns related to locating in Cheyenne and recruitment as 
there are perceptions that issues and biased attitudes exist locally, and their desire is for every staff 
member to be treated with respect.  It was also noted that some military members stationed here 
have experienced harassment by local businesses.  It was acknowledged that laws exist on the 
federal level and opposing views were voiced on enforcement of those laws.  Reporting for 
Committee of the Whole, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Johnson.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by adding “political affiliation” to the appropriate sections 
throughout the ordinance that reference the protected classes, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Dr. 
Aldrich advised the purpose of the amendment is due to past physical attacks and vandalism at 
local political party’s headquarters.  It was noted other states have included political affiliation in 
similar ordinances.  Upon inquiry, Kris Jones, City Clerk, advised it would be appropriate to 
include “political affiliation” to other sections in the ordinance for clarification of referenced 
protected groups and noted that was the intention of the amendment.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” all members of the governing body.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend Section 9.08.120 
by removing Section A-3.  Motion died due to lack of a second.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend 
Section 9.08.120 by adding a new Section E – “This Ordinance will be considered null and void 
when applied to citizens in legitimate exercise of rights enumerated under Article 1, Sections 2, 3, 
20, and 36 of the Wyoming Constitution and the First Amendment of the United States 
Constitution.  Motion died due to lack of a second.   References were made to previous Supreme 
Court cases involving constitutional protected class cases, some of which involved religious 
affiliations and beliefs.  Appreciation was expressed to everyone who has put effort into the 
ordinance, and it was further noted that everyone in the community should be protected.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”.  
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.20, 
Cemeteries, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne 
pertaining to Cemetery fees.  Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
repealing UDC 5.4.7, UU – Urban Use Overlay District, and creating UDC 5.6.9, UU – Urban Use 
Overlay District – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of

referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook)

RESOLUTION – Adopting the revised City of Cheyenne, Wyoming Investment Policy

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and file the Wyoming

RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Sweetgrass, 9th Filing, a Replat of all of Lot 1, Block 4, Sweetgrass, 1st Filing situated in the N1/2 
of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located south of East Murray Drive west and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive).  
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning, agent for the owner, advised a replat was previously planned 
for a multi-family parcel in the Sweetgrass PUD.  He provided details of the Sweetgrass 
development history noting the family who owned the property supported multi-family 
development and explained that in general the Sweetgrass development has been decades in the 
planning.  Mr. Palma advised the master plan included a 7.68 acre plat for 168 units which is now 
for 116 units which is within the provisions of PlanCheyenne for urban residential stipulations and 
noted the exception is the PUD is unique.  He provided additional information on proposed open 
space, land reserved for public schools and public parks.  Comments and concerns were raised 
regarding proposed density of the project and related anticipated reduction of street infrastructure.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. Palma reported it is anticipated there will be many additional filings relating to 
the continued development of Sweetgrass which will take years to complete.  Following concerns 
and comments regarding setting a precedence on high density and size requirements with the plat, 
Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and Development, advised that every plat filed will be 
considered on its own.  Comment was made that the proposed plat is fully compliant with the PUD 
as it is written today.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Concern was expressed 
relating to 400 people residing in the vicinity if the final plat is approved, possible issues with 
homeowner’s associations relating to maintenance and the challenge to provide adequate parking.  
Motion failed due to a tie vote.  Voting “yes” - Mayor Collins, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne 
and Mr. Roybal. Voting “no” - Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Segrave and Mr. 
White.  
 
 
RESOLUTION – Adopting a fee schedule for Planning and Development projects. Brad 
Emmons, AVI, expressed appreciation to Planning & Development Director Charles Bloom for 
his efforts on the resolution.  Mr. Emmons voiced concern regarding the proposed revised fee 
schedule and the proposed requirement to obtain a property appraisal for right-of-way vacations, 
as currently appraisals are taking approximately 8 weeks to complete.  Mr. White moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Mr. White moved to amend Attachment “A” by substitute dated 
February 22, 2022, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and 
Development, provided details for the proposed substitute which includes a change to the street 
vacation fee and noted it is up to the governing body if they choose to require the proposed 
appraisal requirement and fee.  Motion to amend Attachment “A” carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom provided information on the $50.00 
permit fee explaining it is due to the amount of Planning and Development staff time required to 
perform reviews of numerous permits.  He noted the proposed fee would generate approximately 
$23,000.00 annually.  John Edwards, Saddle Ridge Subdivision, expressed concern on fee 
increases that will have an effect on affordable housing.  Mr. Bloom provided information on staff 
review time on the different types of plats submitted and requested consideration to require a 
higher fee for expedited plats as opposed to preliminary and final plats.  Discussion topics included 
an option of capping the price per lot to $2,500.00, desire to avoid deterring development and 
option to postpone for additional discussion and review.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to March 
14, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of order regarding the motion to 
postpone as it was made on top of the motion to amend.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Stefanie 

Boster advised the motion to amend could either be approved or denied prior to postponement and 
City Clerk Kris Jones advised the motion to postpone was out of order.  Mayor Collins ruled the 
motion to postpone out of order and called for a vote on the motion to amend.  Motion to amend 
failed.  Voting “no”– all members of the governing body.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to March 
14, 2022, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes”– all members of 
the governing body. 
 
 
RESOLUTION – For Governing Body approval of a one-year Bronze Boot Sponsor

to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_35817604.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 3, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in special session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at the Historic Depot, 121 W. 15th Street, and via electronic conference meeting, for 
the purpose of collective labor bargaining with the Cheyenne Professional Fire Fighters, 
International Association of Fire Fighters, Local No. 279.  Present were: MAYOR – Patrick 
Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook (arriving at 6:05 p.m.), Ken 
Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn (arriving at 6:05 p.m.), Dr. Mark Rinne (arriving at 
6:05 p.m.), Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City 
Clerk; Stefanie Boster, City Attorney; Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, and Chief John Kopper, 
Cheyenne Fire and Rescue.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Mayor Collins advised the meeting would be audio recorded and available on Facebook 
live and stated that Wyoming State Statute §16-4-405(a)(x) of the Public Meetings Act allows the 
governing body to meet in executive session to consider or tender offers regarding salaries, wages 
or other terms and conditions of employment during negotiations with the fire fighters union and 
that any motion approved by the governing body to move into executive session during the special 
meetings would be pursuant to this section of statute.    
 
Mayor Collins provided opening comments noting he has been meeting with the Fire 
Department for labor management discussions which have resulted in a better understanding of 
the functions of their department.  Mayor Collins expressed appreciation to Chief John Kopper for 
the work he does with the Union and his representation of the Fire Department.  The proposed 
Ground Rules were agreed upon and signed by the City and Union. [Clerk’s note: City’s 
Spokespersons are: Mayor Patrick Collins, City Attorney Stefanie Boster and City Council 
President Scott Roybal.  Union’s Spokespersons are: Bill Hinz, Chris Thoms and Vincent 
McQueeney.]  
 
Following signing of the Ground Rules by Mayor Collins and Union Spokesperson Chris 
Thoms, the proposed Collective Bargaining Agreement was exchanged between the parties.  
Mayor Collins briefly reviewed the City’s proposal which included changes to the following areas 
and Sections: update cover page; update table of contents; Section 2, Definitions - strikethrough 
on-call language and shift trainer, add Specialty Team definition; Section 4, Terms of Agreement 
– update date of contract; Section 8, Grievances – strikethrough Division Chief language, remove 
Operations, Training, and Prevention; Section 13, Management Rights – add #10 and #11 
pertaining to the City’s right to establish and determine specialized emergency response teams and 
establish job descriptions; Section 15, Staffing – under Company Strength, update equipment 
dispatched to structural fires to sixteen members without an aerial truck and seventeen members 
with an aerial ladder truck; Section 21, Classification of Employment – strikethrough on-call 
Prevention language and update Day Personnel’s Specialty pay; Section 22, Overtime – add Fire 
Chief language to monitor and add overtime conversion for Day Personnel; Section 24, Specialized 

   
Teams – remove this section; Section 43, Longevity – update Day Personnel’s rate and remove 
40-year language, and Section 44, Shift Trainers – remove this section and update signature page.  
Union Spokesperson Chris Thoms briefly reviewed the Union’s proposal which included changes 
to the following Sections: Section 4, Terms of Agreement – update date; Section 15, Staffing – 
under Company Strength, update equipment dispatched to structural fires to sixteen members 
without an aerial truck and seventeen members with an aerial ladder truck; Section 20, Holidays – 
add Juneteenth; Section 21, Wages – add $1.50 an hour base wage increase effective 07/01/2022 
for all members, and Section 23, Working in a Higher Job Rank -  update “acting” language.  Mr. 
Thoms noted the change in Section 23 is similar to specialty pay.  
 
Mr. Segrave moved to meet in executive session, seconded by Mr.Cook.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   Those present in executive session were:  All 
members of the governing body; Stefanie Boster, City Attorney; Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk; 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer; Chief John Kopper, Cheyenne Fire & Rescue; Andrew Worshek, 
Chief of Staff; Jennifer McClelland, City Council Executive Assistant; Briana Ralda-Quinonez, 
City Attorney Legal Assistant, and Michael Skinner, City Public Information Officer. (Clerk’s 
Note: executive session convened at 6:13 p.m.). 
 
 
Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 6:38 p.m.  Union Spokesperson Chris Thoms echoed the importance of the labor management 
discussions that occur throughout the year leading up to negotiations.  Mr. Thoms announced the 
Union’s acceptance of the City’s proposed agreement as written.  Mayor Collins announced the 
City’s acceptance of the Union’s proposed agreement as written.  
 
  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:40 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk, CMC 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
March 11, 2022 
 
 
 
 


--- END OF FILE ---

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO TABLE 5-1: 
ZONING DISTRICT USES IN UDC SECTION 5.1.4, DISTRICTS AND 
USES, AMENDING THE PERMISSIBILITY OF VARIOUS USES.”  
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2. That the intent of this text amendment is to amend the permissibility of uses 
allowed within the City’s zone districts. 
 
Section 3. That on February 22, 2022, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 4. That Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts and Uses 
is amended as shown in Attachment A, consisting of three (3) pages, which is attached hereto 
and incorporated herein. 
  
Section 5. The aforementioned sections of the UDC are hereby amended as set out within 
this ordinance and sections currently existing within the UDC not set out within this ordinance 
remain unchanged. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_88097441.pdf
ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) TO CREATE A COMMON LANDSCAPING PLAN AND 
DEFINITION OF DEVELOPMENT FOOTPRINT.”  
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2. That the intent of this text amendment is to create a common landscaping plan 
and definition of development footprint. 
 
Section 3. That on February 22, 2022, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment with modifications. 
 
Section 4. That Section 1.4.3 of the UDC is amended to add the following definition:  
 
“Development Footprint. The development footprint is the total land area that is 
permanently affected by development activities, and includes buildings, structures, 
hardscape, utilities, roads and parking areas, maneuvering areas, storage areas, areas 
associated with the use on the property, and areas cleared of natural vegetation and 
topsoil.”  
 
Section 5. That Section 6.3.10, is created to read as follows:  
 
“6.3.10 Common Landscape Plan 
As an alternative to individual site landscaping, developments of eighty (80) acres or 
larger in Light Industrial (LI) and Heavy Industrial (HI) zoning districts may submit a 
common landscape plan that would contribute to uniformity of a development’s 
collective appearance. Common landscape plans shall focus on providing landscaping 
that is beneficial to the public, consistent with the general landscape character and 
context of the area, and water conservation. Common landscape plans shall be 
administratively approved by the Director. 
 
a. Submittal Requirements.  Applications for a common landscape plan may be 
initiated by all property owner(s) of all property subject to the common 
landscape plan, or an agent or entity authorized by all property owner(s) 
through mutual agreement or a property owner’s association. 
 
b. Application, Submittal, and Phasing. An applicant shall submit a conceptual 
common landscape plan, prepared by a licensed landscape architect, for the 
entire development based on presumed development footprints for the plan 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:39 am, Mar 04, 2022

 
 
Page 2 of 4 
area. Installation of landscaping may be developed in phases and the 
developer shall submit phasing plan in conjunction with submittal of common 
landscape plan.  Phasing plans shall show general locations of landscaping, 
materials, plantings, groundcover, anticipated development footprints, and any 
transferred points within the plan area.  As development occurs, site / 
landscape plans shall be submitted and reviewed by the City to determine that 
each site meets the intent of the common landscape plan. 
 
c. Common Landscape Plan Standards.  The landscape standards of Chapter 
6.3 of the UDC shall apply, except as set forth below.  
 
1. Required Internal Landscape Area. The Required Internal Landscape 
Area of Section 6.3.3 shall be ten (10) percent of the development 
footprint. Required internal landscaping may be transferred to other areas 
within the area subject to the plan. The use of required internal landscape 
trees within and adjacent to parking areas and as an addition to required 
street trees, is encouraged.  
2. Street Trees. The Street Tree standards of 6.3.6 shall be adjacent to 
arterials, collectors, local streets, and interstates subject to City Forestry’s 
standards for street trees and the Street Design Standards of Section 4.3, 
at the rate of one tree per sixty (60) feet of street frontage.  Street trees 
shall be in the adjacent public right-of-way or seventy-five (75) feet of 
the property line adjacent to a street right-of-way if circumstances unique 
to the site prevent the placement of street trees within the right-of-way. 
 
d. Tree Reduction and Relief of Requirements Allowance. Up to a twenty (20) 
percent reduction of the required number of internal and/or street trees may be 
granted by the Director based on specific site conditions and to best meet the 
development’s collective appearance. If a tree reduction is approved: 
 
1. A per tree monetary donation shall be made by the applicant to the City, 
for every tree reduced, to be deposited in a specific fund for use in 
purchasing and installing trees for the Greater Cheyenne Greenway and 
other City lands, including but not limited to, open spaces, park areas and 
rights-of-way. 
2. The City Forester shall determine the monetary donation per tree yearly on 
January 1st of each year, based on the average wholesale and installation 
cost of trees from three regional wholesale nurseries. This fee shall be in 
effect for the calendar year and be available to the public. 
3. A per tree monetary reduction can count toward the point system as a new 
tree. 
 

 
 
Page 3 of 4 
e. 
Review Criteria. In reviewing, providing comments, and making decisions 
on a proposed common landscape plan, the Director shall use the following 
criteria: 
 
1. The proposed alternative design achieves the intent of the subject design 
standard to the same or better degree than the subject standard; achieves 
the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne to the same or better degree than 
the subject standard; and results in benefits to the community that are 
equivalent to or better than compliance with the subject standard; or 
2. The required landscaping on each lot is not practical because of location, 
topographic, or site constraints while the alternative design can 
accommodate site-specific issues. 
3. The plan represents a logical phasing plan for the development.   
4. Adequate utilities exist to serve the landscape elements detailed in the 
plan. 
5. The plan shall not place an undue fiscal burden on the City or other public 
entities.   
6. An entity has been identified for maintenance of common landscape plan 
elements and any necessary agreements can be executed within sixty (60) 
days of approval. 
7. Incorporates an area of at least eighty (80) contiguous acres.  For the 
purposes of area calculation, intervening rights-of-way shall be included in 
the measurement.   
8. The plan is prepared by a licensed landscape architect. 
 
f. Amendments and Alterations. Amendments to the common landscape plan 
may only be initiated with the consent of all property owner(s) of all property 
subject to the common landscape plan or an agent or entity authorized by all 
property owner(s) through mutual agreement or a property owners’ 
association.  
g. Recording and Approval. Following approval of a Common Landscape Plan, 
the Director shall sign the plan and the applicant shall then record the plan on 
each affected lot or parcel in the office of the County Clerk and be responsible 
for any associated fees. The applicant shall provide a copy of the recorded 
plan to the Director prior to issuance of any building permits. 
h. Abandonment of Plan. In the event the applicant wishes to abandon the 
approved Common Landscape Plan, the Director must determine that 
abandonment of the plan is not detrimental to the plan area and that the 
applicant has taken reasonable and necessary steps to mitigate any non-
conforming site conditions on existing sites in the plan area. Requests for 
abandonment may be initiated by the Governing Body, the Director, by all 
property owner(s) of all property subject to the common landscape plan, or an 
agent or entity authorized by all property owner(s) through mutual agreement 

 
 
Page 4 of 4 
or a property owner’s association. Upon Director determination that 
abandonment is acceptable, the Director shall sign any necessary releases to 
be filed with the office of the County Clerk.”  
 
Section 6. The aforementioned sections of the UDC are hereby amended as set out within 
this ordinance and sections currently existing within the UDC not set out within this ordinance 
remain unchanged. 
 
Section 7. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication. 
 
FIRST READING: February 14, 2022 
SECOND READING: February 28, 2022 
THIRD AND FINAL READING: March 14, 2022 
 
 
 
________________________________________ 
PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR 
 
(SEAL) 
ATTEST: 
 
 
__________________________________ 
KRISTINA F. JONES, CITY CLERK 


--- END OF FILE ---

ORDINANCE NO.____________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, LAND LOCATED EAST OF SOUTHWEST DRIVE, SOUTH 
OF I-80.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING:
Section 1.
That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows:
(a)
That Tru-Grit Land Development, LLC, owners of:
a portion of Tract 1, H & B Tracts, Laramie County, Wyoming, more 
particularly described as follows:
beginning at a point being the northeast corner of said Tract 1, monumented 
with a 3" brass cap WYDOT right-of-way marker; thence S.03 deg 38' 03" W., 
along the east line of said Tract 1, a distance of 483.00 feet; thence S.80 deg 
21’ 10" W., a distance of 1533.88 feet; thence N.00 deg 06’34” E., a distance 
of 37.81 feet; thence S.79 deg 29’47"W., a distance of 444.72 feet to a point on 
the west line of said Tract 1, monumented with a survey cap stamped "DMH 
PE & LS 558"; thence N.14 deg 33' 35" E., along said west line, a distance of 
464.54’ to the northwest corner of said Tract 1, monumented with a 3" brass 
cap WYDOT right-of-way marker; thence N.73 deg12' 59” E, along the north 
line of said Tract 1, a distance of 334.52’ to a 3" brass cap WYDOT right-of-
way marker; thence continuing along said north line, along a curve, concave to 
the southeast, with a radius of 5455.39 feet, curve delta 16 deg 27' 00", arc 
length of 1566.28’ and chord bearing of N.81 deg 17' 58"E., a distance of 
1560.91’ to the POB.
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; and
(b)
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as Exhibit 
A, said map prepared by Cotton D. Jones, a Professional Land Surveyor, licensed
in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 3333; and
(c)
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and
(d)
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and
(e)
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and other 

services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; and 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned MU Mixed 
Use by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entire annexed 
area. 
 
Section 2.
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
Section 3.
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to the 
official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
Section 4.
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
Section 5.
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

AGENT:
Steil Surveying Services, LLC
Casey Palma
OWNER:
Tru Grit Land Development LLC
PURPOSE:
To allow future development
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00004
February 14, 2022
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 48.6 acres of land for future development  most likely residential. 
The County Assessor presently shows the tract of land as a portion of a platted  lot, one ownership parcel. 
The land is currently developed in the County with one single-family home. A zone change is proposed 
concurrently with this annexation. Both applications are anticipated to come to final consideration before the 
Governing Body at the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County MU Mixed Use to the City AG Agricultural zone. The purpose of this 
annexation is to allow future urban development 
 future zone change and 
platting action is anticipated to create urban development patterns. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned MU Mixed Use in the County. The property is concurrently proposed to 
be annexed and rezoned to the City AG Agricultural zone. The City AG zone is intended for low-intensity 
operations, including agriculture, and is appropriate as a holding zone until such time urban 
development patterns are finalized. Future development is anticipated to be largely residential with 
possibly a mix of commercial uses (according to the comprehensive plan designation for the area). 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation

UDC-22-00004
February 14, 2022
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the occupants of 
future development once it is built. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to 
City Fire and Police and sanitation services. The property, if occupied, will also be required to be 
connected to City sanitary sewer instead of having septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. For all intents, the property is surrounded by the City (the only non-annexed area is 
the railroad right-of-way). The contiguity on three sides and the proximity to surrounding urban-
style development makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The development of 
this property will benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the social and 
economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. City sewer lines exist in the vicinity and both water and 
sewer lines would likely be extended with the development of the property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 75.1% contiguous, along all but the eastern edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 

UDC-22-00004
February 14, 2022
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on January 14, 2022. The 
public hearing for this annexation is scheduled for February 14, 2022. Twenty (20) business days 
prior to the announced public hearing is January 14, 2022. The earliest confirmation of delivery 
was dated January 18, 2022. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
Policies within PlanCheyenne encourage the maximization of existing infrastructure and the reduction of 
County pockets. This property is near existing urban infrastructure (although water lines would require 
significant extension), and the annexation would effectively eliminate a County pocket. 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with a mix of urban residential and lower-intensity commercial uses. These areas 
are expected to develop with full access to urban services  such areas are best served by being within 
City limits. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
- 
New septic systems will not be allowed. 
- 
Water and sewer are not immediately adjacent to this parcel and extensions will be needed 
with development. 
- 
Annexation will require future development to comply with City-adopted regulations 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Aerial, Current City Limits, and Future Land Use Map 
2. Submitted Application 
3. Applicant-submitted annexation map 
4. Supporting photos

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM 
COUNTY MU MIXED USE TO AG AGRICULTURAL FOR LAND LOCATED 
EAST OF SOUTHWEST DRIVE, SOUTH OF I-80.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on February 7, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a 
public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as a portion of Tract 1, H 
& B Tracts, Laramie County, Wyoming, more particularly described as follows:
beginning at a point being the northeast corner of said Tract 1, monumented with a 3" brass cap 
WYDOT right-of-way marker; thence S.03 deg 38' 03" W., along the east line of said Tract 1, a 
distance of 483.00 feet; thence S.80 deg 21’ 10" W., a distance of 1533.88 feet; thence N.00 deg 
06’34” E., a distance of 37.81 feet; thence S.79 deg 29’47"W., a distance of 444.72 feet to a point 
on the west line of said Tract 1, monumented with a survey cap stamped "DMH PE & LS 558"; 
thence N.14 deg 33' 35" E., along said west line, a distance of 464.54’ to the northwest corner of 
said Tract 1, monumented with a 3" brass cap WYDOT right-of-way marker; thence N.73 deg12' 
59” E, along the north line of said Tract 1, a distance of 334.52’ to a 3" brass cap WYDOT right-
of-way marker; thence continuing along said north line, along a curve, concave to the southeast, 
with a radius of 5455.39 feet, curve delta 16 deg 27' 00", arc length of 1566.28’ and chord bearing 
of N.81 deg 17' 58"E., a distance of 1560.91’ to the POB.
is hereby changed to AG Agricultural.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

UDC-22-00004-01
February 7, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Tract 1 (less 22.38 acres), H and B Tracts, Laramie County, Wyoming to 
AG Agricultural as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review 
criteria for approval, with the following condition: 
1. 
change map.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County MU Mixed Use to AG Agricultural 
for 48.6 acres. The land is undeveloped. The impetus behind the proposed zone change is to give land a City 
zone in concert with an annexation.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary and some existing 
infrastructure exists to the west. There are also opportunities to tie into infrastructure. 
Development will require infrastructure extensions and improvements.   
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District

UDC-22-00004-01
February 7, 2022
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-use Residential on the Future Land 
Use Map. The optimal zoning for the proposed future land use designation is MUR Mixed-use 
Residential, although a variety of residential and lower-intensity commercial zones may also be 
appropriate. 
 
While the proposed AG zone is not consistent with the land uses identified by the Future Land 
Use Map, the AG zone is 
finalized, and such zoning would not have an adverse effect on the potential for the property to 
develop in accordance with the plans and policies of the comprehensive plan in the future.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The existing zoning to the southwest is AR, while to the east, west, and north there is LI zoning, 
and to the south is MUB zoning. There is also PUD zoning to the west. The property to the west 
zoned LI is the animal shelter. Properties zoned LI to the east are across railroad tracks, and the 
area zoned LI to the north is undeveloped. The areas zoned PUD and MUB are also 
undeveloped. The AG zone is compatible with the areas zoned AR, and, as a holding zone, is 
compatible with the other undeveloped areas surrounding it.  
 
The future land uses anticipated by the Comprehensive Plan for this area anticipate higher-
intensity, urban-style uses. While the proposed AG zone is not consistent with the Future Land 
development occurs. 
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current County MU zone allows a wide variety of uses; however, most of those uses would 
require water and sewer service, and the South Cheyenne District is unable to service this 
parcel. Therefore annexation to the City and the establishment of City zoning would be required 
to establish the types of uses typically considered by the current County zone. While AG zoning 
within the City only allows low density residential and lower-intensity agricultural uses, it is also 
appropriate as a holding zone until more urban-style development is ready to occur. 
    
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The standards of Article 4 have limited applicability to the proposed AG zone, as AG zoned land 
is highly limited in allowed uses and is intended to have limited infrastructure improvements, if 

UDC-22-00004-01
February 7, 2022
any. By zoning the land as AG, the property would likely remain undeveloped until future zoning, 
subdivision, and other development actions occur. The standards of Article 4 would be required 
to be met with those future development actions.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The AG zone is intended to develop with primarily agricultural uses or to leave land in a natural 
state. This zone prohibits most of the typical urban-style uses.  The AG zone is applicable to 
areas where significant natural lands or open spaces are to be protected, or areas designated 
Agriculture/Rural or Rural Residential by the Future Land Use Map. The AG zone is also 
applicable as an interim or holding zone for areas that may be further developed in the long 
term, but where minimal development activity in the interim is acceptable and will not 
prematurely establish development patterns. 
 
This property is anticipated to develop with urban-style uses in the future. However, the AG 
zone is appliable as a holding zone and is intended to leave the land in an undeveloped state  
which would allow future rezoning and development of the property when development is 
appropriate. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The applicant is seeking the proposed zone change in concert with an annexation. While the 
subject property is anticipated to develop with urban-style uses in the future, establishing 
specific development patterns at this time may be premature. Interim agricultural style uses or 
very-low density residential uses may be appropriate until such time as the land is ready for 
urban-style development. 
  
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 7. 
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00004-01
February 7, 2022
8)
Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the clarity of the application. The 
application, the neighbor letter, and discussions with the applicant all point to a request for AG zoning; 
. The map should be corrected for clarity. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The zoning map 
amendment application is proposed in concert with an annexation. While the property is anticipated to 
develop with urban-style uses in the future, the AG zone is applicable as a holding zone. Staff 
recommends approval of the zoning map amendment as submitted by the applicant. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant.  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications (Staff 
Recommendation).  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County MU to AG. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS TO HI HEAVY INDUSTRIAL FOR 
LAND LOCATED NORTH OF CAMPSTOOL ROAD, WEST OF 
PROGRESS CIRCLE.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1,
Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on February 7, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Tracts 20-22, Rocky Mountain Industrial Park Addition Third Filing; and Lots 1-2, 
Block 1, Rocky Mountain Industrial Park Fifth Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed to
HI Heavy Industrial.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as 
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00005: Rocky Mountain Industrial Park Zone Change 
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 23.4 acres of land from CB 
Community Business to HI Heavy Industrial   
LOCATION:
North of Campstool, west of Progress Circle
OWNER:
Cheyenne Leads
ZONING:
CB Community Business
FUTURE LAND USE:
Industrial
PURPOSE:
To allow outdoor storage use
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development 
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00005
February 7, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Tracts 20-22, Rocky Mountain Industrial Park, 3rd Filing; and Lots 1-2, 
Block 1, Rocky Mountain Industrial Park, 5th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming to HI Heavy Industrial as shown 
in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from CB Community Business to HI Heavy 
Industrial for 23.4 acres. The land is undeveloped. The impetus behind the proposed zone change is to allow 
the industrial use of Outdoor Storage on at least one of the subject parcels.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development.   
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Industrial, and the zoning classification of 
HI is one of the optimal zones for areas so designated. 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Industrial  
CB (Community Business) 
Undeveloped 
North 
Urban Residential 
County A-2, County HI 
Undeveloped 
South

(warehousing)

(warehousing)

UDC-22-00005
February 7, 2022
Policies within PlanCheyenne encourage industrial areas to be located outside of activity centers 
and with ready access to regional transportation routes. This area supports these objectives. 
 
Based on the policies and future land use designation of the subject property, HI zoning is 
appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Nearby property to the north and west are largely undeveloped and across a railroad track. 
Property to the south is zoned HI and developed with a large-scale distribution facility. Property 
to the east is zoned CB and contains both offices and warehousing-type facilities. The 
development patterns in this area are compatible with the proposed HI zoning. While CB allows 
more commercial-style development, the CB region to the east is surrounded by HI zoning. HI 
zoned land is required to have buffers to areas zoned CB.  
 
The comprehensive plan encourages industrial uses and zoning for all of the property between 
the railroad and I-80 in this area of the community. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property is currently zoned CB Community Business. CB zoning requires building design 
standards and is intended for regional-serving commercial and service uses. The proposed 
zoning of HI has no design standards and allows more intense uses which are intended to be 
buffered from the standard residential/commercial areas of the community. While the property 
has the ability to develop according to the standards of either zone district, the subject property 
is more likely to develop with industrial-style uses than with commercial service style uses. A HI 
zone is required to provide a buffer to CB zoned areas, but the parcels in question should have 
enough area to be able to provide the intended buffer. 
 
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The standards of Article 4 regarding block size and connections are not really applicable to 
industrial areas; and due to the adjacent railroad and highway, north-south options for 
connections are very limited. The area is already platted and no replatting actions are currently 
proposed with this zone change. The existing subdivision pattern better matches HI zone 
expectations than CB zone expectations.  

UDC-22-00005
February 7, 2022
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The HI zone is intended for uses of very high intensity which should be able to be isolated and 
buffered from other uses. This zoning is most appropriate in areas with access to major regional 
transportation infrastructure and areas designated Industrial in the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
While HI zoning does not have design standards, other development standards are still 
applicable. The property zoned HI should be able to provide the required landscape buffer to 
the adjacent CB zoning, and certain HI uses are required to have buffers from streets. There 
have been no official applications submitted in concert with this zone change application, and HI 
zoning will allow a variety of more intense uses  should the potential development action that 
prompted this zone change not be executed, the HI zone is appropriate at this location. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No recommendations were received through the review process.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00005
February 7, 2022
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received suggesting the proposed zoning was compatible 
with the comprehensive plan. Comments were also received noting that a buffer would be required to 
the adjacent CB zoned parcels. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The HI zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use designation for the subject property and surrounding 
development patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant  (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
ifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from CB to HI. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_79270736.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE CREATING A NEW SECTION 9.08.120,  MALICIOUS 
HARASSMENT BASED ON RACE, COLOR, RELIGION, ANCESTRY, 
SEX, SEXUAL ORIENTATION, GENDER IDENTITY AND EXPRESSION, 
NATIONAL ORIGIN, DISABILITY, OR POLITICAL AFFILIATION OF 
CHAPTER 9.08, OFFENSES AGAISNT PUBLIC PEACE AND DECENCY, 
OF TITLE 9, PUBLIC PEACE AND WELFARE, OF THE CODE OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.” 
 
 
BE IT HEREBY ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That a new Section 9.08.120, malicious harassment based on race, color, 
religion, ancestry, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity and expression, national origin, 
disability, or political affiliation, of Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and Decency, of 
Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created 
as set out in this ordinance. 
 
9.08.120 
Malicious harassment based on race, color, religion, ancestry, sex, sexual 
orientation, gender identity, gender expression, national origin, disability, or 
political affiliation. 
 
A. 
It shall be unlawful and an offense for any person to maliciously and with the 
specific intent to intimidate or harass another person because of that person’s race, color, religion, 
ancestry, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, gender expression, national origin, disability, or 
political affiliation, commit any of the following acts: 
 
1. 
Assault, batter, assault and batter of another person; 
 
2. 
Damage, destroy, vandalize, deface, trespass upon, or steal any real or 
personal property of another person; or 
 
3. 
Threaten, by word or act, to do any act prohibited by subparagraph 1. or 2. 
of this subsection if there is reasonable cause to believe that such act will occur. 
 
 
B. 
It shall be unlawful and an offense for any person to maliciously and with specific 
intent to incite or produce, and which is likely to incite or produce, imminent violence, which 
violence would be directed against another person because of that person’s race, color, religion, 
ancestry, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, gender expression, national origin, disability, or 
political affiliation, make or transmit, or cause or allow to be made or transmitted, any telephonic, 
computerized, or electronic message. 
 
 
C. 
It shall be unlawful and an offense for any person to maliciously and with specific 
intent to incite or produce, and which is likely to incite or produce, imminent violence, which 
violence would be directed against another person because of that person’s race, color, religion, 
ancestry, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, gender expression, national origin, disability, or 
By Stefanie Boster at 12:49 pm, Mar 04, 2022

 
Page 2 of 2 
political affiliation, broadcast, publish, or distribute, or cause or allow to be broadcast, published, 
or distributed, any message or material. 
 
 
D. 
Any person violating any provision of subsection A., B. or C. of this section shall 
be guilty of a misdemeanor on a first offense and, upon conviction, shall be punished pursuant to 
the general provisions of Section 1.24.010 of the city code, excluding costs, fees, and assessments, 
or by both such fine and imprisonment. 
 
 
Section 2.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon passage and 
publication.

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) REPEALING UDC 5.4.7, UU – URBAN USE OVERLAY DISTRICT, 
AND CREATING UDC 5.6.9, UU – URBAN USE OVERLAY DISTRICT.” 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
 
Section 2.  That the intent of this text amendment is to facilitate Urban and Infill 
Development in the City of Cheyenne. 
 
 
Section 3.  That on February 22, 2022, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
 
Section 4. That sub-section 5.4.7, Urban Use Overlay, is deleted in its entirety. 
     
Section 5. That sub-section 5.6.9, Urban Use Overlay, is created to read as follows:  
 
“ a. Intent. The UU overlay district is intended for Urban and Infill Development. The UU 
district is intended to transition and step down from the urban core and other urban 
areas of Cheyenne into the surrounding neighborhoods.  
b. Applicability. The UU overlay district is applicable to various lands envisioned for 
urban development. A base zone district of P, MUB, or MUR must be in place in order 
for the UU overlay to apply. 
c. Context. This district is most appropriate in areas with existing or future urban 
development patterns. 
d. Lot and Building Standards. The following lot and building standards shall apply 
and replace the lot standards for the underlying district for all uses:
