e. Permitted and Restricted Uses.  This section shall apply when the underlying zoning 
district is P, MUR or MUB.    
1. Permitted Uses 
a.  All uses allowed in the underlying zoning district are allowed as indicated in 
Table 5-1, except as specifically prohibited or allowed in subsections 5.4.7.e.2 
below. 
2. Prohibited Uses: 
The following Uses are prohibited in the UU overlay base zone districts:  
a. Impoundment Yards 
b. Outdoor Sales 
c. Automobile Service Repair 
d. Automobile Service Station – Limited and General 
3. Allowed by Conditional Use in all UU overlay base zone districts: 
a. Moderate Industrial 
 
f.  Site and Building Design Standards. The following development standards 
shall supplement those contained in this Code.  Wherever possible, and if not 
exempted, development shall comply with the standards of Article 5 and Article 6 of 
the UDC, and the standards of this section; however, in the event of a direct conflict 
the standards of this section shall govern. 
1. 
Parking, Lot Access and Circulation.  The minimum required Automobile 
Parking standards of UDC 6.2.4.a shall not apply to property within the UU district.  
(a.) Bicycle Parking. All new development or changes of use that require a Site 
Plan per section UDC 2.2.3.b. shall provide 2 bicycle parking spaces per 
2,000 square feet of gross floor area.  
1.Required bicycle parking is permitted in the ROW so long as the 
parking does not interfere with the 5’ required ADA pedestrian zone. 
2. Applicant will have the ability to provide cash-in-lieu for bicycle 
parking to the shared biking facilities fund. 
2. 
Commercial Building Design Standards. Development shall comply with the 
standards of UDC 6.7. with the following modifications:   
(a.) West Edge Area. Properties included in the UU overlay district that fall 
within the West Edge district, located between 24th Street and the Union 
Pacific Railroad from north to south and Carey Avenue to Missile Drive from 

 
 
3 
east to west, shall incorporate the design standards of UDC 6.7; or, as 
alternative compliance, shall incorporate the design guidelines from Chapter 5 
of the 2016 West Edge Area Wide Plan into any development that requires a 
Site Plan Review per UDC 2.2.3.b. 
(b.) Adaptive Reuse. Adaptive reuse is encouraged in the Urban Use Overlay 
District.  Developments that incorporate an existing structure designated as a 
significant building in any overlying subarea plan, such as the 2016 West 
Edge Area Wide Plan, shall receive an exemption from the standards of UDC 
6.7. Buildings that receive this exemption shall incorporate design guidelines 
from the applicable plan. 
(c.) Alternatives. Minor deviations from the standards may be evaluated by the 
Director to meet the intent of the small scale commercial and mixed-use 
design standards of the UDC through an alternative design to allow a site-
specific plan that is qualitatively equal to or better than the strict application 
of a design standard specified in this code. This procedure is not intended as a 
substitute for a variance or administrative adjustment or a vehicle for relief 
from standards of this Code.  To grant an alternative design, the director shall 
find either:  
1.The proposed alternative design achieves the intent of the subject 
design standard to the same or better degree than the subject standard; 
achieves the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne or the applicable plan 
to the same or better degree than the subject standard; and results in 
benefits to the community that are equivalent to or better than 
compliance with the subject standard; or  
2.The required improvement would be ineffective because of locational, 
topographic, or site constraints while the alternative design can 
accommodate site-specific issues.   
3. Street Trees. Shall comply with the standards of UDC 6.3.6. 
4. Street Furnishings and Projections into ROW. May be placed in the ROW so 
long as the furnishing does not interfere with the 5’ required ADA pedestrian zone. 
All street furnishings and projections into the ROW shall be shown and approved by 
the Site Plan process per UDC 2.2.3.b. 
(a.) Street furnishings shall include benches, tables, chairs, decorative patio 
fencing, hosting stands, or other items permitted by the Director.  
(b.) Other projections into the ROW may include awnings, patios, or other 
projections permitted by the Director. 
 
Section 6.  That the aforementioned Section of the UDC is hereby amended as set out 
within this ordinance and sections currently existing within the UDC not set out within this 
ordinance remain unchanged. 
 
 
Section 7.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 

 
 
4 
 
Section 8.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
February 22, 2022 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, repealing UDC 5.4.7. UU – Urban Use Overlay District and 
creating UDC 5.6.9. UU – Urban Use Overlay District. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLN-22-00003 
PREPARED BY: 
Mark Christensen, AICP, Planner II, Planning and Development 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
to repeal UDC 5.4.7. UU – Urban Use Overlay District and create UDC 5.6.9. UU – Urban Use Overlay 
District, noting the request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND AND DISCUSSION: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of Plan Cheyenne, and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The City of Cheyenne Plannning and Development Department drafted the Urban Use Overlay (UU) 
District in Spring of 2021 as part of on-going efforts to revitalize the West Edge District. The overlay was 
adopted into the Unified Development Code on June 28, 2021. Staff looked to mapping the UU Overlay 
District in late 2021 but noted that the regulations have been placed in the incorrect location in the UDC. 
Additionally, staff noted the verbiage in the Intent, Applicability, and Context limited the potential 
implementation of the UU Overlay District. This current effort remedies both situations. The proposed 
ordinance moves the UU Overlay District to 5.6.9. in the Special Purpose and Overlay Districts section of 
the UDC and proposes new verbiage in the Intent, Applicability, and Context of the District to allow more 
areas of the community to take advantage of the flexibility the UU Overlay District provides. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
 
The proposed amendments include the creation and amendments of the following sections taken from 
the drafted ordinance: 
 
Section 4.  That sub-section 5.4.7, Urban Use Overlay, is deleted in its entirety. 
 
Section 5. The subsection 5.6.9, Urban Use Overlay, is created to read as follows: 
 
a. “Intent. The UU overlay district is intended for Urban and Infill Development adjacent to the 
Central Business District of Downtown Cheyenne. The UU district is intended to transition and 
step down from the urban core and other urban areas of Cheyenne into the surrounding 
neighborhoods. 
b. Applicability. The UU overlay district is applicable to various lands envisioned for urban 
development adjacent to the Central Business District. A base zone district of P, MUB, or MUR 
must be in place in order for the UU overlay to apply. 
c. Context. This district is most appropriate in areas with existing or future urban development 
patterns. 
d. Lot and Building Standards. The following lot and building standards shall apply and replace the 
lot standards for the underlying district for all uses: 
TYPE UU: Urban Use Overlay District Lot and Building Standards 
Lot Area 
No minimum or maximum 
Lot Frontage 
n/a 
Setbacks (Minimum)

0’ minimum, 10’ maximum, or 
context of surrounding properties 
Side 
0’ minimum, no maximum side 
setbacks 
0‘ minimum, 10’ maximum street 
side setbacks  
5’ minimum and 15’ maximum on lot 
lines fronting and adjacent to the 
Reed Avenue ROW 
Rear 
0’

Buildings shall not have a pinnacle 
height that exceeds an elevation of 
6,150’ in the West Edge District.  
Total Lot Coverage (max.) 
90%, 100% if site provides a street 
furnishing or projection as listed in 
5.4.7.f.4. 
 
e. Permitted and Restricted Uses.  This section shall apply when the underlying zoning district is P, 
MUR or MUB.    
1. Permitted Uses: 
a. All uses allowed in the underlying zoning district are allowed as indicated in Table 
5-1, except as specifically prohibited or allowed in subsections 5.4.7.e.2 below. 
2. Prohibited Uses: 
The following Uses are prohibited in the UU overlay base zone districts:  
a. Impoundment Yards 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
b. Outdoor Sales 
c. Automobile Service Repair 
d. Automobile Service Station – Limited and General 
3. Allowed by Conditional Use in all UU overlay base zone districts: 
a. Moderate Industrial 
f. Site and Building Design Standards. The following development standards shall supplement 
those contained in this Code.  Wherever possible, and if not exempted, development shall 
comply with the standards of Article 5 and Article 6 of the UDC, and the standards of this 
section; however, in the event of a direct conflict the standards of this section shall govern. 
1. 
Parking, Lot Access and Circulation.  The minimum required Automobile Parking 
standards of UDC 6.2.4.a shall not apply to property within the UU district.  
(a.) Bicycle Parking. All new development or changes of use that require a Site Plan per 
section UDC 2.2.3.b. shall provide 2 bicycle parking spaces per 2,000 square feet of 
gross floor area.  
i. Required bicycle parking is permitted in the ROW so long as the parking 
does not interfere with the 5’ required ADA pedestrian zone. 
ii. Applicant will have the ability to provide cash-in-lieu for bicycle parking to 
the shared biking facilities fund. 
2. 
Commercial Building Design Standards. Development shall comply with the standards 
of UDC 6.7. with the following modifications:   
(a.) West Edge Area. Properties included in the UU overlay district that fall within the 
West Edge district, located between 24th Street and the Union Pacific Railroad from 
north to south and Carey Avenue to Missile Drive from east to west, shall 
incorporate the design standards of UDC 6.7; or, as alternative compliance, shall 
incorporate the design guidelines from Chapter 5 of the 2016 West Edge Area Wide 
Plan into any development that requires a Site Plan Review per UDC 2.2.3.b. 
(b.) Adaptive Reuse. Adaptive reuse is encouraged in the Urban Use Overlay District.  
Developments that incorporate an existing structure designated as a significant 
building in any overlying subarea plan, such as the 2016 West Edge Area Wide Plan, 
shall receive an exemption from the standards of UDC 6.7. Buildings that receive this 
exemption shall incorporate design guidelines from the applicable plan. 
(c.) Alternatives. Minor deviations from the standards may be evaluated by the Director 
to meet the intent of the small scale commercial and mixed-use design standards of 
the UDC through an alternative design to allow a site-specific plan that is 
qualitatively equal to or better than the strict application of a design standard 
specified in this code. This procedure is not intended as a substitute for a variance 
or administrative adjustment or a vehicle for relief from standards of this Code.  To 
grant an alternative design, the director shall find either:  
i. The proposed alternative design achieves the intent of the subject design 
standard to the same or better degree than the subject standard; achieves 
the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne or the applicable plan to the same or 
better degree than the subject standard; and results in benefits to the 
community that are equivalent to or better than compliance with the 
subject standard; or  
ii. The required improvement would be ineffective because of locational, 
topographic, or site constraints while the alternative design can 
accommodate site-specific issues.   
3. Street Trees. Shall comply with the standards of UDC 6.3.6. 
4. Street Furnishings and Projections into ROW. May be placed in the ROW so long as the 
furnishing does not interfere with the 5’ required ADA pedestrian zone. All street 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
furnishings and projections into the ROW shall be shown and approved by the Site Plan 
process per UDC 2.2.3.b. 
(a.) Street furnishings shall include benches, tables, chairs, decorative patio fencing, 
hosting stands, or other items permitted by the Director.  
(b.) Other projections into the ROW may include awnings, patios, or other projections 
permitted by the Director.” 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or is 
necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is consistent with the principles and policies within PlanCheyenne. 
Specifically, the proposed amendment addresses the following policies and principles: 
 
• 
Policy 1.1.B: Flexibility to Respond to Market Demands. 
The UU Overlay text amendment will provide flexibility with zoning regulations to promote 
infill development, adaptive reuse, and redevelopment of an important area in the City of 
Cheyenne. The flexibility should incentivize the market to consider developing in the West 
Edge. 
 
• 
Policy 1.4.A: Infill Opportunities 
The Urban Use Overlay text amendment provides the opportunity for infill development 
through regulations that promote flexibility, but also consider the character of the area in 
which they are located. 
 
• 
Policy 1.4.C: Downtown Revitalization and Mix of Uses 
The Urban Use Overlay can be applicable to the MUR, MUB, and P zone districts in or adjacent 
to downtown. As the Urban Use Overlay is applied to the West Edge it is anticipated to 
revitalize the area and further mix uses in the area. Staff anticipates the development of the 
West Edge will greatly benefit the downtown as a whole. 
 
• 
Policy 1.4.D: Infill and Revitalization Incentives 
The Urban Use overlay achieves several items that are mentioned in this policy; such as 
reducing parking minimums, providing flexible dimensional standards, promoting higher 
densities than currently permitted, and providing flexibility in design requirements for 
utilizing existing buildings. 
 
• 
Policy 2.3.A: Mix of Housing Types 
The Urban Use Overlay will lead to the West Edge being zoned MUB or MUR; each of which 
provide ample opportunities for a variety of housing. 
 
• 
Policy 3.2.B: Active Uses in the Street Level 
The Urban Use Overlay promotes active uses on the street level by permitting various street 
furnishings and projections into the Right of Way. 
 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
• 
Principle 4.3: The Cheyenne Area will build a safe, multimodal transportation system that 
consists of streets, sidewalks, bicycle facilities, and transit 
The Urban Use Overlay promotes bicycle infrastructure and pedestrian amenities through 
increased bicycle parking and an active pedestrian zone. 
 
• 
Policy 5.1.A: Historic Building Restoration 
The Urban Use Overlay promotes building reuse and restoration with flexible site design and 
building placement requirements for utilizing existing buildings.  
 
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-602 (a):  
The governing body shall specify how regulations, restrictions and the 
district boundaries are to be determined, established, enforced, 
amended, supplemented or otherwise changed. 
Assessment:  
State statute states that the governing body shall specify how regulations 
regarding zoning are established and enforced.  The governing body 
possesses the authority to amend regulations and therefore can amend 
the Unified Development Code to reflect this text amendment. 
 
WYO 15-1-601 (a):  
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(i) Regulate and restrict the: 
(D) Density of the population 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, 
industry, residence or other purposes. 
Assessment:  
This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code for 
the purposes of regulating the location of buildings and density of the 
population; thus, fulfilling the requirements of this statute.  
 
WYO 15-1-601(d):  
All regulations shall be made: 
(ii) With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the character 
of the district and its peculiar suitability for particular uses; 
(iii) With a view to conserving the value of buildings and encouraging the 
most appropriate use of land throughout the city or town. 
Assessment: 
This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code for 
the purposes of encouraging redevelopment and infill development in 
the City of Cheyenne with respect to the existing character of the districts 
to which these regulations are applied. Furthermore, these regulations 
contain provisions for height regulations to preserve views of existing 
prominent structures. Regulations of this nature are allowable under 
state statue and may be modified by the Governing Body.    
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment will promote general health, safety, and public welfare by reinvesting in the 
community and promoting walkable, mixed-use development. Furthermore, it is anticipated that 
this amendment will promote much needed housing development in the community.  
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
The Urban Use Overlay district provides an effective framework for redevelopment in near-downtown 
neighborhoods in Cheyenne, specifically in the West Edge District. However, staff feels this flexibility 
should also be available to more areas of the community. The proposed amendments allow for more 
utilization of the UU Overlay District and relocate the UU Overlay District to the correct location in the 
UDC. Staff is satisfied that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.  
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code to repeal UDC 5.4.7. 
UU – Urban Use Overlay District and create UDC 5.6.9. UU – Urban Use Overlay District, noting the 
request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM P 
PUBLIC DISTRICT TO CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS FOR LAND LOCATED 
SOUTHWEST OF AND ADJACENT TO THE INTERSECTION OF CLEVELAND 
AVENUE AND U.S. 30.” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, 
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
Section 2. That on February 22, 2021, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a 
public hearing to review the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance. 
 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as Lot 1, Block 1, Wynn 
Addition, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming is hereby changed to CB Community Business. 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official 
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases. 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-22-00030:  1720 Cleveland Ave. Zone Change  
 
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 2.18 Acres of land from P Public to CB 
Community Business

South and east of Lincolnway, south and west of Cleveland Avenue

City of Cheyenne

PREPARED BY: 
Mark Christensen, AICP, Planner II - Development  
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-22-00030 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot 1, Block 1, Wynn Addition to CB Community Business as shown in 
the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval, with the 
following condition: 
1. The Future Land Use Map for the area be amended to Mixed-use Commercial prior to (or 
concurrently with) the zone change application.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from P Public to CB Community Business for 
City-owned property. The land is developed with a fire station, which is proposed to be relocated. The 
purpose of the proposed zone change is to allow for future commercial redevelopment of the City-owned 
property once the fire station is relocated. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING:

UDC-22-00030 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. PlanCheyenne also encourages infill development.   
 
The Future Land Use designation for this property is currently Public; however, it is anticipated 
that the designation for this area of the community will be changed to Mixed-use Commercial 
(MU-C). The MU-C designation recognizes that the location will likely redevelop by private 
enterprise once the fire station is relocated. Furthermore, the MU-C designation matches the 
Future Land Use designations to the north and west. 
 
Based on the policies and proposed future land use designation of the subject property, 
Community Business zoning is appropriate at this location. Community Business is an acceptable 
zone within the MU-C Future Land Use designation as it fulfills the commercial component of 
the designation, 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1, with conditions. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Several of the surrounding properties to the north are zoned Community Business and are 
developed with commercial uses. Furthermore, the properties to the west that are zoned 
Mixed-use Business Emphasis and are also developed with commercial uses. The surrounding 
properties to the north and west carry a Mixed-Use Commercial Emphasis which anticipates 
predominantly commercial development. Lastly, the location adjacent to E. Lincolnway further 
reinforces the suitability of the site for commercial uses. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The Public zone is crafted for use by public entities, such as the City of Cheyenne. With the 
planned relocation of the fire station, it is anticipated the City will dispose of the subject 
property to a private entity for commercial development. The Community Business zone will 
afford the future developer a variety of opportunities for redevelopment compared to the 
current Public zone.  
   

UDC-22-00030 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
Community Business zoning typically anticipates larger suburban-style blocks, consistent with 
the current site at 2.18 acres. The existing pattern is compatible with the proposed zoning and 
staff is comfortable that the subject property will meet the requirements of Article 4 should 
future redevelopment require a subdivision process. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The Intent of the Community Business district is for retail, employment, service, and civic uses 
that may serve a variety neighborhoods. The Applicability of the district states that the CB 
district is appropriate in areas with moderate- or large-scale businesses can be anticipated. The 
location of the subject property along a major arterial, E. Lincolnway, and size of the lot, 2.18 
acres, yield conditions conducive to moderate-scale commercial businesses that will serve the 
surrounding neighborhood.  
 
The Community Business zone is required to meet the Large Scale Commercial Design Standards 
of 6.8. Staff is confident that the future development of the subject property will be able to 
meet these requirements.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The existing property has adequate area and site conditions to meet the standards of the 
Community Business district. There are no current applications for development of the subject 
property and staff is satisfied that the various uses permitted in the CB district will be able to 
meet the applicable standards at the subject property. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
Staff presented the zoning map amendment to the Project Advisory Team on Thursday February 
10. No comments were received. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
 

UDC-22-00030 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The CB zone is 
appropriate based on the proposed future land use designation for the subject property and 
surrounding development patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant.  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications (Staff 
Recommendation).  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from P to CB, contingent on the proposed 
future land use map amendment for the subject property to Mixed-use Commercial emphasis.

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS TO CB COMMUNITY 
BUSINESS 
FOR 
LAND 
LOCATED 
SOUTHEAST 
OF 
THE 
INTERSECTION OF COLLEGE DRIVE AND CHARLES STREET.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1,
Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on March 7, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as: the north ½ of Tracts 7 & 8, Sunnyside Addition, 2nd Filing (less an 8,325sqft portion 
to State Highway 212), City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, is hereby changed to CB 
Community Business.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as 
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION
MARCH 7, 2022
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00029: College & Charles
Zone Change 
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 1.9 acres of land from MUB Mixed-
use Business Emphasis to CB Community Business   
LOCATION:
Southeast of the intersection of College Drive and Charles Street
APPLICANT:
National Care Development LLC
AGENT:
Mark Stec 
OWNER:
Albert N. Simpson
ZONING:
MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis
FUTURE LAND USE:
Mixed-use Commercial
PURPOSE:
To allow construction of a commercial business
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of the N½ of Tracts 7 and 8 (less 8,325sqft to St Hwy), Sunnyside 
Addition, 2nd Filing to CB Community Business as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that 
the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MUB Mixed-use Business to CB 
Community Business for 1.9 acres of land. The land is currently vacant. The property is anticipated to be 
developed in the future with a medical care facility and drive through facility on the southern portion of the 
site. The northern portion of the site has no proposed uses at this time.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development (although it is not the 
intent of the plan to change existing, stable neighborhoods).   
 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use

Site

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial. Areas to the west 
and south are also designated Mixed-use Commercial. The areas to the north and east are 
designated Urban Residential. The intersection of College and Pershing is designated as an 
Activity Center. The subject property is roughly 1,0
at intersection. 
 
According to comprehensive plan the optimal City zone for the 
 future land 
use designation of Mixed-use Commercial is MUB. However, CB zoning provides for commercial 
development and is generally encouraged near activity centers.  
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, CB Community Business zoning is appropriate at 
this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
This property, and surrounding property to the east and west, are currently zoned with mixed-
use zoning designations. Although these nearby properties are currently developed with largely 
single-family uses, all of them could redevelop with commercial uses (compatible with the 
proposed CB zoning)
-use 
Commercial, which would also anticipate future commercial development.  
 
The property to the south is zoned with a high-density residential PUD. The PUD is already 
bordered on south by a CB-zoned area. This property to the south has the future land use 
designation of Mixed-use Commercial.  
 
The property to the north is zoned CB and is only partially developed with commercial uses.  
 
The future land use designation to the north and east is Urban Residential, however these 
properties are zoned with commercial and mixed-use zones, and the future land use map is 
 the location adjacent to a major thoroughfare 
and mixed-
acceptable.  
 
The proposed CB zoning would match existing zoning to the north and would be compatible 
with the development patterns in the vicinity. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property is currently zoned MUB, which requires buildings to be brought closer to the street 
and have a pedestrian-oriented feel. Although Charles Street my provide some opportunity to 
be built with a pedestrian-centered development, College Drive is a major, WYDOT right-of-way 
with more limited opportunity to provide the streetscape anticipated by MUB zoning. The CB 

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
zone is intended for more automobile-centric development. The MUB zone allows a variety of 
commercial and residential uses, while the CB zone is more commercial focused. This location 
along a major thoroughfare is more likely to develop with  commercial-style uses. The 
specifically-proposed use associated with this zone change is allowed in both zone districts. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The existing block is within the anticipated size for regional commercial development associated 
with CB zoning. However, it is much larger than anticipated for mixed-use development. Mixed-
use development anticipates smaller blocks than regional commercial centers, so the proposed 
CB zoning is compliant with block sizes, while the current MUB zoning is not. The block is largely 
already developed and limited options are available to create meaningful street network 
connections. No additions to the street network are proposed.  
 
There are no existing approved accesses to the subject property, and it is unlikely WYDOT would 
allow another access off of College at this location (no matter the zoning). Development of the 
property would require access off of Charles or a shared access with an adjacent property.  
 
Infrastructure (streets and utilities) exist in the area, but improvements may be required with 
development. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB zone is intended for larger commercial uses serving the community as a whole and is 
applicable in activity centers. Internal circulation between adjacent sites is encouraged. The CB 
zone applies commercial design standards. 
 
This property is adjacent to CB zoning (to the north), and the proposed zone change is 
effectively an expansion of an existing CB zone district which meets the intent and applicability 
of the CB zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific development proposed in concert with this rezoning is a commercial business which 
would be allowed in either the current or proposed zone. The purpose of the zone change is to 
modify the required setbacks and building design standards. Should the proposed development 
not be executed after approval, there are also a number of commercial uses which would be 
eligible to be established at this location. 

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the CB 
zone at this location and concerns about access off of College. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The CB zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and surrounding development 
patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
ifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
 

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MUB to CB.

1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Maps

ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE CREATING A NEW SECTION 9.01.010, DEFINITIONS 
OF TITLE 9, PUBLIC PEACE AND WELFARE, OF THE CODE OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.” 
 
 
BE IT HEREBY ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That a new Section 9.01.010, Definitions of Chapter 9.01, Title 9, Public Peace 
and Welfare, of the code of the City of Cheyenne is hereby created as set out in this ordinance. 
 
A. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this title is to set out definitions for misdemeanor 
offenses within the city.  This title compliments and does not supersede any Wyoming statutes.  
The city is authorized to prosecute violations of city ordinance and Wyoming statutes, pursuant to 
Wyo. Stat. § 5-6-101 and Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-103(a)(xli) 
 
B. 
Definitions.  The terms defined in Title 9 Public Peace and Welfare shall be as 
follows:  
 
1. 
“Assault” shall be defined and interpreted consistent with Wyo. Stat. § 6-2-
501(a). 
 
2. 
“Batter” or “Battery” shall be defined and interpreted consistent with Wyo. 
Stat. § 6-2-501(d). 
 
3. 
“Harass” shall be defined and interpreted consistent with Wyo. Stat. § 6-2-
506(a)(ii). 
 
 
Section 2.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon passage and 
publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_19173302.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
Subject: A resolution adopting a fee schedule for planning and development projects 
Date: 
February 14, 2022  
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a fee schedule for development-related applications in the Planning and 
Development Department.   Pursuant to section 1.3.7 of the Unified Development Code, fees are 
adopted by resolution.      
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral 
 
February 14, 2022 
• 
Finance Committee 
 
 
February 23, 2022 
• 
Final Reading consideration 
 
February 28, 2022 
 
Summary: 
 
Fees related to applications within the Planning and Development Department were last updated over 
23 years ago on October 25, 1993. The current fees do not adequately cover costs associated with 
development actions and the proposed fees are designed to recover most costs associated with 
development actions.  
 
Staff began working on the revised fee schedule in early 2021 and in developing the fee schedule, the 
following factors were considered: 
• 
Fees of other communities in the region 
• 
Staff management and review time  
• 
Publication costs 
• 
Legal advertisement costs 
• 
Office overhead (10% of staff management and review)  
• 
Data storage and access (Innoprise / Citizen Access) 
• 
Number and complexity of item 
• 
Compliance with requirement to make specific applications 
• 
Degree in which the specific application is a public service 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

Fees of other communities in the region 
Staff’s initial task was to review peer nearby jurisdictions to determine if our fees needed adjustment. 
Communities reviewed included Casper, Fort Collins, Gillette, Kimball, Laramie, Loveland, Timnath, and 
Wellington.  Staff also looked at the nearby counties of Albany, Campbell, Laramie, Larimer, Natrona, 
and Weld. It was noted that most communities had different types of fees, methodologies, and 
application classifications that prevented a simple and straightforward comparison.  One thing was for 
certain though, Cheyenne’s development-related fees were typically the lowest in the region.  For 
example, a preliminary plat in Greeley had an application fee of $3,000 + $10 per lot and Casper’s 
preliminary plat fee was a flat $1,000 compared to our $175 fee.  Site Plans in Casper ranged between 
$600-$2,000 and Loveland were between $800-$2,400 compared to our $100 fee.  This triggered a need 
to investigate the costs associated with development review.        
Staff Management and Review Time 
This factor included surveying all employees included in the development review process. Utilized were 
the average of reported hours per project multiplied by position’s average hourly salary plus taxes, 
worker’s comp, benefits, etc. Review time was averaged for all project types.  For example, if three 
items of one type were reviewed over the period of a year by three planners, we would utilize the 
average time all planners took to review that project and account for their average hourly costs to 
determine a typical rate.     
Publication Costs 
The department has worked to reduce publication costs over the course of the year in working with 
other departments to limit the publication of approved ordinances to the minimum necessary under 
state statute.  Still, several applications (Annexation and Street Vacation) do require publication of the 
full ordinance which generally ranges between $300 and $600 each. Ordinances that may be published 
by title and summary only have been reduced to a cost of approximately $50 each. This fee schedule 
includes publication costs associated with approved ordinances.         
Legal Advertisement Costs 
The department has reduced costs associated with legal advertisements over the course of the year by 
consolidating advertisements for projects and removing irrelevant information. Annexations require 
multiple legal advertisements and specific information average approximately $180 while regular 
Planning Commission items average $30 each.  This fee schedule includes publication costs associated 
with the specific development applications.       
Office Overhead (10% of Staff Management and Review) 
Included as a cost to process an application, was a 10% fee for office overhead ranging between $40 and 
$150, and averaging $66.73 per project.  In 2021, 273 applications would have been subject to this fee, 
and it is estimated that at the current rate it would produce approximately $18,217.29 in annual 
revenue to capture overhead costs. This was not applied to building permit, sign permits, and several 
other application types.  This element of the fee will offset office furniture, supplies, equipment, 
printers, etc. 

Data storage and Access (Innoprise / Citizen Access) 
Presently the City utilizes Harris’s ERP Community Development module (Innoprise/CommDev).   The 
Department represents 1/11th of the Help Desk service totaling a proportionate cost of $529.45 
annually. The Planning and Zoning Module has an annual cost of $11,649.72 which has been equally 
divided into all project types utilizing this system for planning and zoning purposes.  This software is 
used for project tracking, project review, citizen access, and record retention.  The total cost for 
development-related use of this software and access to the help desk is $12,179.17 annually. If a new 
system is implemented, staff will request to modify the fee schedule once again to reflect any cost 
changes.   
Number and complexity of item 
Staff evaluated all types of applications that were received in the department and their level of 
complexity and knowledge necessary to review/complete.  Frequent applications included Site Plans 
(74), Zoning Letter requests (57), Administrative Adjustments (42), as well as action required on items 
forwarded for review by the Building Division including specific building permits (537) and sign permits 
(104). Several repetitive projects were given a flat fee, like Building Permits, and others were given a 
quantitative based fee, like sign permits which are proposed to be calculated on a $10 per sign basis. 
More complex projects oftentimes warranted slight bumps in a fee due to the expertise needed while 
other ones warranted a bump downward and could be considered a public service.      
Compliance with Requirement to Make Specific Applications 
Several City processes have been problematic to adequately capture.  For example, Home Occupations 
exist throughout the City, however we only have record 229 home occupations having been approved 
since 2012.  We are certain that more exist, but do not want to create a fee that would discourage 
voluntary compliance and present itself as a barrier.  Likewise, temporary signage runs rampant 
throughout the City. The Development Division manages all temporary signs, however the only ones 
that are typically reviewed are for temporary placement of signs by nonprofits and community 
organizations at designated locations within City parks. This fee was set at zero to not penalize the 
people lawfully erecting temporary signage which is very few.  Additionally, temporary signs are not 
tracked in our data base and implementation of a nominal fee would likely create more steps, 
complicate a process, and add more work associated with that process resulting in a loss of productivity 
and a negative monetary effect on the department.       
Degree in which the specific application is a public service 
A large portion of the City Planning and Development Department is to provide effective, and efficient 
professional planning services to the community.  In 2021 the department received 229 inquiries and 
hosted 129 preapplication meetings.   These services represent 24.8% of the development-related tasks 
managed annually by a planner. Inquiries typically are informational requests from developers or 
residents inquiring about allowances on their property or a prospective property.  Pre-application 
meetings are intended to identify any major red flags with a proposed development and most all 
involved in the development review process attend those meetings on a weekly basis. Pre-application 
meetings have no fee and represent an approximate $1,200 value to the developer. Staff believes that 
these meetings clarify expectations for future development and ultimately reduce potential increased 
review time, development delays, and staff time spent on applications once formally submitted.  

Current Fee Schedule: 
Below is the current fee schedule that was derived from Resolution No. 3451 which was adopted on 
October 25, 1993. Several additional fees have been added over time including Telecommunication 
Towers and Special Use Permits.   Those remain unchanged in the new fee schedule.   
Proposed Fee Schedule: 
The proposed fee schedule is attached to the Resolution as Attachment A.   Only two fees are being 
carried over from the previous fee schedule - Telecommunication Towers and Special Use Permits. As 
previously indicated in this memorandum the fees were derived by considering a variety of factors.  
Attached to this memorandum is an attachment labeled “Application Cost Summary” summarizing the 
costs associated with specific development review.  Staff notes that the initial subdivision calculations 
were based on lot quantity ranges, not a base fee plus fee per lot basis.  Upon evaluation of lots platted 
with subdivision types, staff determined that a base plus per lot fee resulted in essentially the same 
cost-recovery but more equitably distributed the financial burden of a subdivision review to developers 
based on their plat size.  Below is a summary of the lots associated with subdivision types.

For example, under this scenario, the typical Final Plat application would be $1,915 dollars [$700 base 
fee + $1,215 (81 lots x $15)]. The attached sheet showed a cost of $3,000 for any subdivision over 35 lots 
which has since been revised to a per lot basis. 

Telecommunication Safe Harbor Amounts: 
 
Telecommunications are regulated by the Federal Communications Commission (FCC), and they have 
established specific rates by order at which telecommunication providers can be charged for 
development applications and annual leases.  The safe harbor amounts are: 
• 
$500 for a single up-front application that includes up to five small wireless facilities, with an 
additional $100 for each small wireless facility beyond five,  
• 
$270 per small wireless facility per year for all recurring fees, including any possible ROW access 
fee or fee for attachment to municipally owned structures in the ROW, and  
• 
$1,000 for nonrecurring fees for a new pole.  
 
The FCC views these amounts as safe harbors because it believes they are low enough that no carrier 
would challenge them if they were imposed unilaterally in a local government’s regulations. Nothing in 
the FCC’s overarching order prevents a local government from charging higher fees. However, under the 
FCC's framework, if a carrier files a lawsuit challenging the fees imposed by a local government, the 
burden would be on the local government to demonstrate that the amount is a reasonable 
approximation of its costs and that its costs are reasonable. The FCC did not specify a methodology for 
calculating cost, or what expenses could be included. To this date, we have not had specific applications 
to analyze exact costs and have opted to keep our existing fees for large macro-cell towers and adopt 
the safe harbor amounts for small cell and distributed antenna systems (DAS).  
 
Projected Revenue Comparison to Expenses: 
 
The Planning and Development divisions are comprised of seven employees with three employees 
mostly responsible for processing development-related requests.  The division does have overlap into 
the Planning Division, Parks and Greenway Division, and Office Manager and Planning and Development 
Director positions. Total salaries plus all taxes and benefits for the three primary planners are projected 
to total $232,037.64 in FY2023 based on current funding.  Projected revenue based on this fee schedule 
is $255,955.00 which should account for the planers responsible for current development review and a 
portion of the overflow to the others within the department, including the Planner II Long Range, Parks 
and Greenway Manager, Office Manager, and the Director.  Revenue in 2021 was approximately 
$43,025.    The Department is seeking the Senior Planner Position be reinstated which will cost 
approximately $114, 900 for salary plus benefits.   Addition of the new staff member will likely result in 
additional hours to review individual applications and give existing staff more time to focus on individual 
projects and eliminate potential for error or oversites.   
 
The entire department’s anticipated FY 2023 payroll budget is $895,917.80 (with Senior Planner added) 
($780,994.00 with no new positions) compared to FY 2022’s $741,573.16 payroll budget. Staff will 
continue to monitor costs associated with development review and may ask for further adjustments in 
the future.   
 
Staff Recommendation: 
   
Staff is recommending the Governing Body adopt the resolution to establish a new Planning and 
Development Fee Schedule.

RESOLUTION NO. 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION ADOPTING A FEE SCHEDULE FOR PLANNING AND 
DEVELOPMENT PROJECTS." 
WHEREAS, on January 23, 2012, the Governing Body approved Ordinance 3943 creating 
the Unified Development Code (UDC); and   
WHEREAS, on October 25, 1993, the Governing Body, jointly with the County 
Commissioners, adopted Resolution 3451 creating a schedule of fees related to development and 
subdivision applications; and  
WHEREAS, the costs of conducting business has increased and the established fees do not 
reflect costs associated with development actions or the services offered by the City; and 
WHEREAS, pursuant to UDC Section 1.3.7 the City may charge fees to defray some or all 
of the cost of processing land development applications; and   
WHEREAS, adoption of a fee schedule is necessary to defray the costs associated with 
processing land development applications; and   
WHEREAS, staff has conducted internal research and surveys to measure costs associated 
with development, including but not limited to, costs associated with staff review time, legal notice, 
ordinance publication, data storage, development related software, and data storage; and 
WHEREAS, staff recognizes that some services of the department are a public service in the 
interest of the public safety, health and welfare of the Cheyenne community and fees to defray total 
costs may not be necessary and appropriate and may be nominal to encourage compliance; and   
WHEREAS, a fee schedule has been created and in attached to this resolution as Attachment 
A consisting of one (1) page which is attached hereto and incorporated herein.  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the fee schedule shown in Attachment A of this resolution 
is hereby adopted and all previous development fees are hereby replaced.    
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this resolution shall become effective March 31, 
2022, and shall apply to all applicable applications filed on or after the date. 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS 28TH DAY OF FEBRUARY 2022. 
Patrick J. Collins, Mayor 
(Seal) 
ATTEST: 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
By Stefanie Boster at 9:57 am, Feb 09, 2022

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001

(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
Subject: Substitute Resolution    
Date: 
March 7, 2022  
 
Background:  
Attached is a substitute resolution with a modified attachment A.   The changes incorporated are:  
1. A requirement for staff review of the fee schedule every five years (Councilman Johnson request).  
2. Removal of the appraisal cost fee from the street/alley vacation by ordinance application.   
3. A $2,500 limit has been established for preliminary and final plat applications.       
4. A $5,200 limit has been established for expedited plat applications.  This is the sum of the preliminary and 
final plat applications plus a $200 fee for staff prioritization and the convenience of a quicker review.     
5.  
Vacation Appraisal Fee Removed: 
After hearing public comments at the February 28th City Council meeting, staff agrees that it could cause undue 
and unpredictable delays associated with a street/alley vacation application.  Additionally, receipt of this fee has 
not been included in the annual revenue projections since applications occur infrequently.  Typically, no more than 
one application is filed per year, if at all.    
 
Platting Application Fee Caps: 
Summarized below are when a platting action would max out on fees (Max Out Scenario) and what an application 
fee would be for a typical application based on the average number of lots platted in 2021 (Average Lots Platted).

DEVELOPMENT PROJECTS." 
 
WHEREAS, on January 23, 2012, the Governing Body approved Ordinance 3943 creating 
the Unified Development Code (UDC); and   
 
WHEREAS, on October 25, 1993, the Governing Body, jointly with the County 
Commissioners, adopted Resolution 3451 creating a schedule of fees related to development and 
subdivision applications; and  
 
WHEREAS, the costs of conducting business has increased and the established fees do not 
reflect costs associated with development actions or the services offered by the City; and 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to UDC Section 1.3.7 the City may charge fees to defray some or all 
of the cost of processing land development applications; and   
 
WHEREAS, adoption of a fee schedule is necessary to defray the costs associated with 
processing land development applications; and   
 
WHEREAS, staff has conducted internal research and surveys to measure costs associated 
with development, including but not limited to, costs associated with staff review time, legal notice, 
ordinance publication, data storage, development related software, and data storage; and 
 
WHEREAS, staff recognizes that some services of the department are a public service in the 
interest of the public safety, health and welfare of the Cheyenne community and fees to defray total 
costs may not be necessary and appropriate and may be nominal to encourage compliance; and   
 
WHEREAS, a fee schedule has been created and in attached to this resolution as Attachment 
A consisting of one (1) page which is attached hereto and incorporated herein.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the fee schedule shown in Attachment A of this 
resolution is hereby adopted and all previous development fees are hereby replaced.    
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this resolution shall become effective March 31, 
2022, and shall apply to all applicable applications filed on or after the date. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the attached fee schedule shall, at minimum, be 
reviewed by Planning and Development Department staff every five years hereafter and 
amendments to the fee schedule shall be brought forward to the Governing Body as necessary.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_74016893.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION TO AUTHORIZE THE APPLICATION FOR THE 
CONTINUATION GRANT IN THE AMOUNT OF $318,375.00 FROM THE 
CORPORATION FOR NATIONAL AND COMMUNITY SERVICE FOR 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE OFFICE OF YOUTH ALTERNATIVES 
FOSTER GRANDPARENT PROGRAM.” 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne Office of Youth Alternatives Foster Grandparent 
Program is a not-for-profit organization that depends on grants, donations, and local support; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Foster Grandparent Program provides opportunities for senior service to 
meet unmet needs and assign individuals, 55 and older, to schools and human service agencies 
that serve youth under the age of 21 having special or exceptional needs or with conditions or 
circumstances identified as limiting their academic, social, or economic development; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Corporation for National and Community Service is a Federal 
Governmental agency that awards grants to assist grantees in carrying out a national service 
program as authorized by the Domestic and Volunteer Service Act of 1973, as amended (42 
U.S.C. Chapter 22); and  
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne Office of Youth Alternatives Foster Grandparent 
Program has will apply for the continuation grant in the amount of Three Hundred Eighteen 
Thousand Three Hundred Seventy-Five Dollars ($318,375.00) from the Corporation for National 
and Community Service; and 
 
WHEREAS, the statutory match for the grant is waived and the Office of Youth 
Alternatives shall provide the required local match in the amount of Thirty-One Thousand, 
Eight-Hundred Thirty-Eight Dollars ($31,838.00) through State, Local and other funding.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
1. 
That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in support of the Office of 
Youth Alternatives Foster Grandparent Program and the application for the continuation grant 
from the Corporation for National and Community Service in the amount of Three Hundred 
Eighteen Thousand Three Hundred Seventy-Five Dollars ($318,375.00).  
 
 
2. 
That the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne is hereby designated as the authorized 
representative of the City to act on behalf of the Governing Body on all matters relating to this 
grant and is authorized to execute the grant agreement and associated documents. 
 
 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 10:21 am, Feb 18, 2022

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_45431759.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION PERMITTING THE POSSESSION OF OPEN 
CONTAINERS WITHIN THE DOWNTOWN DISTRICT BOUNDARIES 
FOR THE “TALES & ALES” PUB CRAWL TO BE HELD ON APRIL 2, 
2022.” 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne Governing Body has amended Section 5.12.110 of the 
Municipal Code to allow for the possession of open containers within the downtown district 
boundaries between the Thursday preceding Memorial Day to the Monday of Labor Day of each 
calendar year; however, the periods of time, as well as the boundaries of special areas or districts, 
may be established and/or modified by resolution adopted by the Governing Body; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority (DDA) requests the 
possession of open containers within the boundaries of the Downtown Development Authority 
(DDA) district be permitted for the purpose of hosting the “Tales & Ales” Pub Crawl to be held 
on Saturday, April 2, 2022, from 3:00 p.m. to 10:00 p.m.; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, shopping locally creates a multiplier effect through direct, indirect, and 
induced spending which boosts the local economy and creates more local wealth and jobs; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, a number of studies illustrated on the website of American Independence 
Business Alliance (AMIBA) have shown a 48% return to the community occurs when you spend 
locally; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the event organizers will comply with any and all laws, rules, and regulations 
to ensure the safety of the community; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, adherence to the following requirements are required for participation by 
alcohol vendors and consumers when open containers are permitted as designated herein: 
 
 
1. 
The only containers for alcoholic beverages in the special district downtown area’s 
public spaces, for the day specified herein, are plastic cups.  Glass containers are strictly prohibited.   
 
 
2. 
No outside alcoholic beverage, wine, or malt liquor may be brought into or 
consumed within the open container area; a violation of this provision is a violation of the City’s 
open container prohibition and potentially other state or local statutes or codes. 
 
 
3. 
Nothing herein shall be interpreted as authorizing the possession of open containers 
of alcoholic liquor or malt beverages in or on motor vehicles. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the possession of open containers within the boundaries of the 
Downtown Development Authority (DDA) district is permitted for the purpose of hosting the 
“Tales & Ales” Pub Crawl on Saturday, April 2, 2022, during the dates and times designated 
herein. 
 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 12:52 pm, Feb 24, 2022

RESOLUTION NO. ________________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION RENAMING ERNSTE DRIVE TO RICHARD 
MARTIN DRIVE.” 
 
 
WHEREAS, on August 12, 2019 the Harmony Valley First Filing, final plat was 
approved by the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, on September 1, 2020 the Harmony Valley First Filing, final plat was 
recorded with the Clerk of Laramie County as Record No. 787188, Book 11, Page 185; 
and 
 
 
WHEREAS, on April 23, 2021, the City received a request from Edward J. Ernste, 
Manager Tru-Grit Land Development LLC requesting renaming of Ernste Drive to Richard 
Martin Drive; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with Section 12.08.020 of the City of Cheyenne 
Municipal Code, the City Engineer may name and number streets and avenues.  The City 
Engineer has reviewed the proposed street name change that is the subject of this 
resolution, and recommends approval of said street name change; and 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the forgoing right of way “Ernste 
Drive” as shown on the Harmony Valley First Filing, final plat recorded with the Clerk of 
Laramie County as Record No. 787188, Book 11, Page 185; is hereby renamed “Richard 
Martin Drive” and the Harmony Valley First Filing 
 
NOW BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED:  
 
1. That the Clerk of Laramie County, Wyoming, in whose office the aforesaid plat 
was recorded, shall cause the renaming right-of-way to be indicated in plain legible 
letters and symbols across the part of the plat modified, by striking through “Ernste 
Drive” and adding the words “Richard Martin Drive” in its stead, and shall make 
reference to the volume and page number on which the resolution renaming the 
right-of-way is recorded.    
2. That the City Engineer shall give written notification of such street renaming to the 
owners of adjacent real property as appearing in the records of the County Assessor 
and, to the United States Postal Service, and to such other persons, agencies, and 
offices that may have a direct interest in the renaming.

AND

ROADWAY 
DESIGNATIONS 
IN

WHEREAS, PlanCheyenne, as amended by PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress 
adopted by the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne by Resolution No. 5573, is the Master 
Plan for the City of Cheyenne and the Comprehensive Plan for portions of Laramie County adopted 
by both the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County Board of County 
Commissioners; and 
 
WHEREAS, Connect 2045: Update to the Master Transportation and Future Land Use 
Plan of PlanCheyenne adopted by the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne by Resolution No. 
6106, is the Transportation Master Plan for the City of Cheyenne and the Comprehensive Plan for 
portions of Laramie County adopted by both the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne and 
Laramie County Board of County Commissioners; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Future Land Use Map is illustrated in Connect 2045 as Map 10 and 
PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress as the Future Land Use Map on page 83; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the intent of the Future Land Use plan is not to predetermine specific land 
uses for individual parcels or specific locations for uses or facilities, but to provide a general 
framework for neighborhoods and activity centers organized around roadway networks and major 
parks and open space; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Charles W. Bloom, Director of the Planning and Development Department 
of Cheyenne, has initiated amendments to the Future Land Use Plan and Transportation map to 
amend Open Space and Parks to Community Business for an area of land located Southwest of the 
intersection of Cleveland Avenue and U.S. 30, correct land use categories of lands throughout the 
City inadvertently applied with previous map adoption, modify roadway designations of specific 
roads that inadvertently applied with previous map adoption, and modify roadway designations in 
accordance with previously approved plans, as shown in Attachment A which is comprised of 
three pages which is attached hereto and incorporated herein; and 
 
WHEREAS, this amendment has been processed in accordance with the procedures 
outlined in PlanCheyenne and Wyoming Statutes § 15-1-503 and § 15-1-505; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendments approved by the Governing Body pursuant to the criteria 
set forth on Page 18 of PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress, as amended; and 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:22 am, Feb 22, 2022

held before the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission on February 22, 2022 and the Commission 
approved the proposed Future Land Use Plan and Transportation map amendment; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendment processes and procedures required by PlanCheyenne, as 
amended, have been followed with regard to the request by Charles W. Bloom to amend the Future 
Land Use Plan and Transportation map for the above-described properties. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE
