CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
02/22/2022 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PLN-22-00002:  Amendments to the Cheyenne Area Future Land Use Plan 
& Transportation Map 
REQUEST: 
A request to change the Land Use category from Open Space and Parks to 
Community Business for an area of land located southwest of the 
intersection of Cleveland Ave and U.S. 30, correct land use categories of 
lands throughout the City inadvertently applied with previous map 
adoption, modify roadway designations of specific roads that inadvertently 
applied with previous map adoption, and modify roadway designations in 
accordance with previously approved plans. 
LOCATION: 
Various Locations 
APPLICANT: 
City of Cheyenne Planning and Development Department and the 
Metropolitan Planning Organization 
OWNER: 
Various Property Owners 
ZONING: 
Various Zoning Districts 
PURPOSE: 
To promote commercial development and account for inadvertent conditions 
currently contained in the Cheyenne Area Future Land Use Plan and 
Transportation Map. 
PREPARED BY: 
Mark Christensen, AICP Planner II 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Resolution amending the Land Use category from Open Space and Parks to 
Community Business for an area of land located southwest of the intersection of Cleveland Ave and U.S. 
30, correct land use categories of lands throughout the City inadvertently applied with previous map 
adoption, modify roadway designations of specific roads that inadvertently applied with previous map 
adoption, and modify roadway designations in accordance with previously approved plans. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
PlanCheyenne (2016 Community Plan) 
• 
Connect 2045: Cheyenne Area Transportation Master Plan 
• 
Cheyenne Area Future Land Use Plan and Transportation Map 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLN-22-00002 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
BACKGROUND AND PROPOSED CHANGES: 
 
The Cheyenne Area Future Land Use Plan and Transportation Map was adopted by the Governing Body of 
the City of Cheyenne on December 14, 2020 with the adoption of Connect 2045: Transportation Update to 
PlanCheyenne. Connect 2045 serves as the transportation element to PlanCheyenne, which is the 
comprehensive plan for the City of Cheyenne. The other elements of PlanCheyenne include the 
Community Plan, last updated in 2014, and the Parks and Recreation Plan, last updated in 2006. The 
amendment procedures for PlanCheyenne are contained within the Community Plan of 2014. 
  
The Future Land Use Plan and Transportation Map serve to guide the future development of the 
Cheyenne area, regarding both future uses in the community and the transportation network that 
serves them. The current plan has been in place for over a year, which prompted the City of Cheyenne 
and Metropolitan Planning Organization to review the document for proposed changes. Several changes 
have been proposed as a result of this audit. These changes include both inadvertent applications of 
land use categories, corrections to right-of-way functional classifications, and land use changes for new 
development opportunities. Specifically, these changes include: 
 
• 
Various requests to amend functional classifications contained in the Transportation Map 
(Attachments 1 and 2) 
• 
A request to change the Land Use category from Open Space and Parks to Community Business 
for an area of land located southwest of the intersection of Cleveland Ave and U.S. 30, 1720 
Cleveland Avenue (Attachment 3) 
• 
A request to change the Land Use category from Mixed-use Commercial Emphasis (MU-C) to 
Urban Residential (UR) for an area of land roughly located between Omaha Road, Ridge Road, 
and E. Lincolnway, more precisely shown in the attachment. This is correcting an inadvertent 
application of the Mixed-Use Commercial Emphasis designation (Attachment 4) 
 
The following analysis of conformance with the review criteria address all three of the requested 
changes.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to approve an amendment to the Future Land Use Plan and 
Transportation Map of the Comprehensive Plan, the Commission must make the following 
considerations outlined in the 2014 Community Plan (page 18).  A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) BENEFICIAL OR ADDRESSES ISSUES - The proposed amendment is beneficial to the community 
or addresses issues not foreseen during the planning process.    
 
The corrections to the Transportation Master Plan functional classifications and the amendment 
to the future land use map from MU-C to UR between Omaha Road, Ridge Road, and E. 
Lincolnway address issues not foreseen during the planning process. Both corrections address 
inadvertent applications of designations for future land use and functional classifications that 
were not identified until after plan adoption. Correcting these will be beneficial to the 
community to support rezoning applications, promote predictability in development patterns, 
and identify the types of roads that will need to be built as the community further develops. 
 
The amendment to change the land use category from OS to MU-C for 1720 Cleveland Avenue 
also address an issue unforeseen during the planning process. During plan adoption, it was 
unknown that the existing fire station would be relocated. When this occurs, the current site will 

PLN-22-00002 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
likely redevelop with commercial uses. This will be beneficial to the community by providing 
more development opportunities along a prominent commercial corridor in the community. 
 
The proposed amendments comply with review criteria 1. 
 
2) COMPATIBLY - The proposed amendment is compatible with the surrounding area, and the 
goals and policies of the Plan.  
 
The corrections to the Future Land Use Map and Transportation Master Plan are compatible to 
the surrounding areas as they address inadvertent applications of functional classifications and 
future land use designations.  
 
Additionally, the current OS designation for 1720 Cleveland Avenue does not promote 
redevelopment of the fire station site. The subject property is adjacent to several properties 
with Mixed-use Commercial and Urban Residential designations. An MU-C designation for the 
subject property will compliment both the commercial and residential uses in the area. The 
proposed amendment is consistent with the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne, as discussed in 
more detail below. 
 
The proposed amendments comply with review criteria 2. 
   
3) IMPACT MITIGATION - The proposed amendment will address and mitigate impacts on 
transportation, services, and facilities.  
 
Transportation: 
The transportation network is not expected to be impacted by the future land use map 
amendments. The Omaha Road MU-C to UR designation is a correction in an area where the 
transportation network is fully built out. The amendment from OS to CB at 1720 Cleveland 
Avenue is not expected to have significant transportation impacts. Any impacts at this site will 
be addressed during the site plan process. 
 
The proposed amendments to the various roads’ functional classifications will enhance 
transportation services in the community. All amendments serve to ensure all current and 
future roads are built to the correct specifications.  
 
Services: 
The proposed changes will not have any adverse effects on provision of services to the impacted 
areas. Service providers, such as the fire department, will be included in platting and site 
planning processes to assess service needs. Furthermore, the correction of functional 
classifications may improve service provision as public improvements will be built to ensure 
adequate transportation networks. 
 
Facilities: 
The proposed Future Land Use Map amendments will likely not require new facilities outside of 
the development area. The functional classification amendments will require new facilities to be 
built when development actions occur. 
 
The proposed amendments comply with review criteria 3. 
 

PLN-22-00002 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
4) SERVICE PROVISION - The proposed amendment will have minimal effect on service provision, 
including adequacy or availability of urban facilities and services, and is compatible with 
existing and planned service provision  
 
Areas impacted by the Future Land Use Map amendments will have a minimal effect on service 
provision as they are corrections and a minor amendment to permit commercial uses. The 
functional classification amendments will improve service provision by “right sizing” the 
improvements to be made to the transportation network.  
 
The proposed amendments comply with review criteria 4. 
 
5) ANNEXATION - The proposed amendment does not jeopardize the City’s ability to annex the 
property. 
 
The subject properties involved in the Future Land Use Map amendments are all currently 
within City limits. The amendments to the Transportation Master Plan functional classifications 
will enhance the ability of areas in the County to annex as the road specifications will be more 
clearly defined when development actions occur.  
 
The proposed amendments comply with review criteria 5. 
 .   
6) URBAN SERVICES AREA - The proposed amendment is consistent with the logical expansion of 
the Urban Services Area. 
 
The proposed amendments will not have any impacts on the Urban Services Area. 
 
The proposed amendments comply with review criteria 6. 
 
7) STRICT ADHERENCE - Strict adherence to the Plan would result in a situation neither intended 
nor in keeping with other key elements and policies of the Plan. 
 
Strict adherence to the Plan may result in unintended consequences. The 1720 Cleveland 
Avenue property will not be able to redevelop under the current OS designation as the current 
rezoning effort will not meet the UDC criteria for zoning map amendments. Furthermore, the 
land use designation and functional classification corrections may result in inadequate 
transportation networks and unintended development that is not compatible with existing 
conditions. 
 
The proposed amendments comply with review criteria 7. 
 
8) PUBLIC WELFARE AND CONSISTENCY – The proposed plan amendment will promote the public 
welfare and will be consistent with the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne and the elements 
thereof. 
 
Staff is satisfied that the proposed amendments will not have any adverse effects on public 
welfare. The corrections will serve to promote adequate transportation in the community while 
also promoting predictability in development for established neighborhoods. Furthermore, the 
application accomplishes the following policies contained within PlanCheyenne for the 1720 
Cleveland Avenue Future Land Use Map Amendment: 
 

PLN-22-00002 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
• 
Policy 1.1.B. Flexibility to Market Demands: This goal notes that the Future Land Use 
Plan does not predetermine all uses on individual parcels and is meant to provide 
flexibility. The future land use map amendment to MU-C for 1720 Cleveland Avenue will 
accommodate existing development without being detrimental to existing conditions. 
• 
Policy 1.4.A. Infill Opportunities: The future land use map amendment will facilitate 
infill opportunities for commercial development.  
 
The proposed amendments comply with review criteria 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The other plan applicable to these amendments is Connect 2045. Connect 2045 is a component 
of PlanCheyenne and is supportive of these amendments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied the proposed amendments meet the review criteria outlined in PlanCheyenne. 
The corrections to the Future Land Use Plan and Transportation Master Plan are necessary to 
correct inadvertent applications of land use categories and functional classifications throughout 
the City and County. Furthermore, the amendment from OS to MU-C at 1720 Cleveland Avenue 
will promote infill and redevelopment along a prominent corridor in the community once the 
existing fire station is relocated. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Approve the Resolution amending the Future Land Use Plan and Transportation Map as 
submitted by the applicant and expanded upon by staff (Staff Recommendation). 
2. Approve the Resolution amending the Future Land Use Plan and Transportation Map subject to 
modifications. 
3. Deny the Resolution to not amend the Future Land Use Plan and Transportation Map.  The 
Planning Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As 
part of the motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Resolution until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation, the Commission shall cite specific 
reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval the Resolution amending the Land Use category from Open Space and Parks 
to Community Business for an area of land located southwest of the intersection of Cleveland Ave and 
U.S. 30, correct land use categories of lands throughout the City inadvertently applied with previous 
map adoption, modify roadway designations of specific roads that inadvertently applied with previous 
map adoption, and modify roadway designations in accordance with previously approved plans. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Memo Outlining Proposed Transportation Map Amendments 
2. Updated Transportation Map 
3. Proposed Future Land Use Map Amendments

MEMO 
 
 
TO: 
Charles Bloom, Planning and Development Director 
FROM: 
Tom Mason - Director Cheyenne MPO  
DATE: 
February 8, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Connect 2045 – Master Transportation Plan Amendments 
 
 
Following a detailed review of the Master Transportation Plan (MTP) in Connect 2045 we have 
found the following recommended amendments. The MPO Office is preparing an update to the 
City’s Official Map which is developed from the newest MTP. Comparisons were made between 
the 2015 Official Map, the 2021 Existing Functional Classification Map and the Master 
Transportation Plan from Connect 2045. These detailed comparisons lead us to find the 
following list of changes we recommend as amendments to the 2020 MTP. Additionally, some of 
the proposed corrections fill-in gaps on GIS line work. 
 
1. Railroad Road (CO 215) from US 30 to Four Mile. Reclassify from a Minor Collector to 
a Major Collector to match the Existing Functional Classification.   
2. Happy Jack Road (WYO 210) from Logistics Drive to West Urban Boundary. Reclassify 
from a Major Collector to a Minor Arterial to match the Existing Functional 
Classification.  
3. South Greeley Highway (US 85) from Colorado State Line to Terry Ranch Road. 
Reclassify from a Minor Arterial to a Principal Arterial to match WYDOT classification 
on the NHS.  
4. Archer Parkway (US 30) from I-80 Service Road to East Bound I-80 on- and off-ramp 
intersection. Reclassify from a Minor Arterial to a Principal Arterial to match the 
Existing Functional Classification. 
5. Converse from Lincolnway to Pershing. Reclassify from a Principal Arterial to a Minor 
Arterial to match the Existing Functional Classification.  
6. Crane Bluff between Point Bluff and Ogden should be reclassified from a Minor 
Collector to a local street and removed from MTP, and Crane Bluff between Ogden to 
Converse is not platted and removed from the MTP.  
7. Point Bluff between Plainview and Wild Bluff should be reclassified from a Minor 
Collector to a local street and removed from the MTP. 
8. Mason Way between Converse and Wal Mart parking lot should be reclassified from a 
local street to a Major Collector to match the Existing Functional Classification. 
9. Oakhurst between Evers to Bishop should be reclassified from a Major Collector to a 
local Street to match the Existing Functional Classification. 
10. Evers between Oakhurst to Brittney should be reclassified from a local street to a Minor 
Collector to match the Existing Functional Classification.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
February 28, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Resolution to Uncommit General Fund Reserves  
 
 
On March 14, 2022, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance Committee 
for consideration to uncommit $329,630 in General Fund Reserves for the purchase of the former 
Hitching Post.   
On July 22, 2019, the Governing Body voted to approve the Land Purchase and Sale Agreement 
between the City of Cheyenne and Victory Real Estate Management LLC regarding Lot 1, Block 
1, P.A.S. Subdivision, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming for economic development 
purposes and committed the use of $329,630 in General Fund reserves to do so.   
In governmental accounting, any formal action of the Governing Body to commit the use of 
reserves requires the same formal action to remove the commitment.  Once this resolution is 
approved, this action will add 2.14 days of operating reserves to General Fund reserves. 
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.  

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION UNCOMMITTING $329,630.00 IN GENERAL FUND 
RESERVES APPROVED TO BE USED FOR THE PURCHASE OF THE 
FORMER HITCHING POST, LOT 1, BLOCK 1, P.A.S. SUBDIVISION BY 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERING BODY ON JULY 22, 2019” 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne is required to follow the Governmental Accounting 
Standards Board (GASB) Statement 54, Fund Balance Reporting and Governmental Fund Type 
Definitions; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne’s General Fund Reserves (Fund Balance) is separated 
into five categories, including 1) Non-spendable Fund Balance, 2) Restricted Fund Balance, 3) 
Committed Fund Balance, 4) Assigned Fund Balance and 5) Unassigned Fund Balance as defined 
in GASB Statement 54; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Committed Fund Balance category is defined as resources that are 
constrained to specific purposes by a formal action such as a resolution or ordinance of the 
Governing Body that also requires formal action at the same level or higher to remove the 
commitment; and 
 
WHEREAS, on July 22, 2019, the City of Cheyenne Governing Body approved the Land 
Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Victory Real Estate Management 
LLC for the purchase of real property described as a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, P.A.S. Subdivision, 
City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, for economic development purposes and 
committed the use of $329,630.00 of General Fund Reserves; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, as the purchase did not occur, the committed General Fund Reserves in the 
amount of $329,630.00 can now be uncommitted and become part of the City’s Unassigned Fund 
Balance to be used for other purposes; and 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that $329,630.00, previously committed on July 22, 2019, is now 
uncommitted from General Fund reserves. 
 
 
 
(THE REMAINDER OF THIS PAGE WAS INTENTIONALLY LEFT BLANK) 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 10:38 am, Feb 28, 2022

PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this ______ day of ________________, 2022. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick J. Collins, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
Attest: 
 
 
 
__________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION
MARCH 7, 2022
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00025: Olivet Cemetery, 4th Filing (Expedited Review)
REQUEST:
A plat proposing platting cemetery plots.
LOCATION:
Northwest of the intersection of Pershing and Airport Parkway.
APPLICANT:
Casey Palma Steil Surveying Services
OWNER:
ZONING:
P Public
PURPOSE:
A plat of previously undivided land, and a replat of previously divided land, to 
create and restructure cemetery plots.
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend approval of Olivet Cemetery, 4th Filing, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body, with the following conditions: 
1. Update the map to reflect the status as a final plat approved by the governing body (instead of 
an administrative plat). 
2. 
 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval  
 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a plat for approximately 1.5 acres of land to create cemetery plots. 
While the division of a cemetery into plots is not a typical subdivision, all other cemetery divisions of record 
have been approved by the Governing Body, including Lakeview, Bethel, IOOF, and the previous portions of 
Olivet located south of this proposed plat. The original Olivet division and the Jewish cemetery division were 
not readily available it the various record repositories.  
UDC; however, as no documents were able to be obtained indicating what (if any) division of Olivet north of 
the current 3rd Filing occurred, City Staff has determined this proposed plat requires Governing Body 
approval. 
The City has a cemetery layout for all cemeteries located in this area of the community, and the area north of 
third filing is blank. Various repositories were investigated by Staff and the applicant and, as of this writing, 
no previous plat has been found. Should the previous document be found, this plat may be withdrawn and 
the procedures guiding administrative plats may be followed. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the plat, the Commission must make 
the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d   a detailed analysis is below. NOTE  as a cemetery, not 
all review criteria are as strictly applicable to this application. 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1)

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne designates this area (and all surrounding 
property) as Public and Quasi-public. Cemeteries are given as an example quasi-public use.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
This cemetery layout provides reasonable access to all plots and is similar in nature to 
surrounding cemeteries. Urban design principles are largely not applicable to cemeteries, and 
the over-arching parcel (as an open space) 
   
 
The application complies with this review criterion, to the extent the criterion is applicable.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Transportation Network: 
The plat proposes dividing an area already owned for cemetery use. The over-arching parcel is 
part of an extra-large block (containing the airport), and connections could be provided through 
the over-arching property. A Greenway connection (linking Airport Parkway and Evans Avenue) 
is proposed in the vicinity, but outside the boundaries of the proposed plat.  
 
Community Facility Fees: 
Due to the public and open-space like nature of a cemetery, this plat is exempt from plat fees. 
 
 
Open Space: 
As a cemetery, this plat is largely entirely open space in nature. 
  
Required Improvements: 
While a form of land division, it is not anticipated that individual cemetery plots would require 
the same level of service as typical developable parcels. Utilities and access are available to the 
over-arching development. No re-alignment of utilities or easements are necessary. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, to the extent the criterion is applicable.  
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
This plat represents one phase of the cemetery  
. No specific 
phasing is proposed within this plat.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
No technical studies were requested or provided in conjunction with this platting action. 
Generally, the subject property with the proposed use will have a negligible impact on traffic 
and drainage.  
 
This review criterion is largely not applicable to this application. 
 
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat is to allow individual plots to be laid out in an over-arching cemetery parcel. 
The over-arching parcel meets the anticipated development pattern of the P zone.  
 
This review criterion is largely not applicable to this application. 
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
. The over-arching 
ownership and maintenance patterns are not anticipated to be modified with this platting 
action. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, to the extent the criterion is applicable. 
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
The surrounding area to the south and west is previously developed with cemetery plots. The 
areas to the north and east are largely vacant, but are anticipated to develop with cemetery 
plots in the future. This action maintains connections to the previously divided areas, and leaves 
reasonable access to the future phases.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
Staff is satisfied the proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, or 
general welfare.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The purposes behind the submittal and approval of a preliminary plat are largely not applicable 
to the division of cemetery plots. 
 
This review criterion is largely not applicable to this application. 
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Conditions are technical in nature, and will be required to be addressed prior to recordation.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As an expedited review, this Final Plat review criterion is covered by the equivalent Preliminary 
Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
Pershing Boulevard Complete Streets 
 
Cheyenne Area On-Street Bicycle Plan and Greenway Plan Update 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. All other plans are largely silent on 
the development of this parcel, anticipating the property to be utilized as a cemetery. The proposed plat 
is in conformance with this expectation. The Cheyenne Area On-Street Bicycle Plan and Greenway Plan 
Update suggested a Greenway connection in this area (connecting Evans and Airport Parkway) 
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. The location of a future Greenway connection in this area (north of the proposed plat). 
2. Inability to find any documentation of a previous action dividing the northern portion of 
the property.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
This application meets all review criteria as filed. 
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body (Staff 
Recommendation).  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. 
s and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for 
approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of Olivet Cemetery, 4th Filing, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
MARCH 7, 2022 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-21-00451: Rocking A, Preliminary Plat (Expedited Request) 
REQUEST: 
A preliminary plat proposing to replat all of Lot 100 and 200, Block 15, 
Sweetgrass 6th Filing and all of Tract U Sweetgrass 2nd Filing in addition to a 
portion of the unplatted land to the east. 
LOCATION: 
South of Murray Road and East of Sweetgrass Drive.  
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning 
OWNER: 
Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC 
ZONING: 
Sweetgrass PUD (Ordinances 4232, 4286) 
PURPOSE: 
A preliminary plat proposing replating All of Lot 100 and 200, Block 15, 
Sweetgrass 6th Filing and all of Tract U Sweetgrass 2nd Filing into 89 Cluster 
Home (Attached) lots. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00451  
 
 
March 7, 2022 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend approval of Rocking A at Sweetgrass Preliminary Plat, subject to staff’s 
recommended conditions, noting that as conditioned, the project meets the review criteria for approval, 
advancing the Final Plat to the Governing Body. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval  
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for a 15.38-acre parcel of land to create 89 lots for 
Cluster Home (Attached) Development. There will be 12 open space lots with two of them being existing City 
owned Greenway. The land is currently vacant.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is zoned as part of the Sweetgrass PUD.  The Sweetgrass PUD is generally described as “a 
±2,349.5-acre community envisioned as a distinctive mixed-use master planned village, integrating 
retail, entertainment, lodging, employment opportunities, and variety of intimately scaled residential 
neighborhoods.”  The district generally allows a broad range of uses. The HDR (High Density Residential) 
land use category is located in close proximity to Laramie County Community College and planned retail 
/ commercial development along College Drive. These parcels are intended to provide for residential 
development to support the student and employee housing needs associated with the community 
college and surrounding community in the form of apartments, townhomes, and more affordable single 
family detached dwellings. To promote greater diversity, lower density development, such as that 
permitted within MDR and LDR districts, is permitted within HDR districts.

UDC-21-00451  
 
 
March 7, 2022 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Expedited Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d and UDC 2.1.3.d a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with a 
residential use. This plat proposes a replat of an existing lot to allow for townhouse 
development.  
 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 2 of PlanCheyenne encourages new residential development to be compatible and 
connected with existing residential neighborhoods. The proposed plat is a replat of an existing 
lot that is adjacent to other residential developments and was planned to be a townhouse 
development. 
 
Foundation 6 of PlanCheyenne encourages the creation of open space and trails and the 
preservation of significant natural resources. The plat proposes dedicated open space lots that 
will be a part of an integrated trail system throughout Sweetgrass along with internal open 
space areas for the residents of the development. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
This is a replat of an existing lot with in Sweetgrass, 6th Filing. Utilities and facilities are already 
within the area due to the existing developments in the area. Additional Public right of way is 
being proposed with this plan. Accesses are spaced properly along Sweetgrass Drive and East 
Murray Road. This parcel is within the HDR zone in Sweetgrass.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Transportation Network: 
The plat proposes replating land Southeast of an existing street network and adding to the 
network. The plat proposes adding four new streets with a connection into the existing 
Sweetgrass Drive and a connection into a portion of East Murray Drive which will be constructed 

UDC-21-00451  
 
 
March 7, 2022 
with this development. The proposed street network is in compliance with the network 
proposed by the Sweetgrass development. The proposed streets are within a narrower right-of-
way than what is typically required by the UDC. 
 
Community Facility Fees: 
Fees were paid with the original plat for Sweetgrass, 6th Filing. The required fees for the 9.5-
acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$3,325 ($350 per acre) 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
$3,150 Credit 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
 
$3,325 ($350 per acre) 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
$3,150 Credit 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$350.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
 
Open Space: 
Open Space parcels are provided on this plat. They are platted as Tracks KK, LL, MM, and various 
lots. These are all labeled as Private Trails/ Open Space. These tracks are part of Sweetgrass PUD 
trail system.  
  
Required Improvements: 
This division can provide all required utilities to the properties within this development.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. The Sweetgrass PUD calls this portion of the 
2,000 plus acre Sweetgrass site, part of phase 1 through 3 of 10.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A preliminary Drainage Report was submitted and reviewed. It was found that the plan meets 
the intent of the preliminary drainage report requirements contained in Article 3 of the 
Cheyenne UDC. A final drainage report specific to this property will be submitted with the EPR.  
  
 

UDC-21-00451  
 
 
March 7, 2022 
Traffic: 
A Traffic Impact Study Letter update was submitted. The proposed platting action was reviewed 
and found to be within the scope of the original Traffic Report based on the updated letter.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat is one of the HDR zones of the PUD. The HDR land use category is located in 
close proximity to Laramie County Community College and planned retail / commercial 
development along College Drive. These parcels are intended to provide for residential 
development to support the student and employee housing needs associated with the 
community college and surrounding community in the form of apartments, townhomes, and 
more affordable single family detached dwellings. To promote greater diversity, lower density 
development, such as that permitted within MDR and LDR districts, is permitted within HDR 
districts. All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
The PUD requires common lots to be owned, developed, improved, managed, and/or 
maintained by homeowners’ associations, architectural review board(s), community 
development districts, and/or similar entities (PUD Section 1, Subsection G). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat is in conformance with the design and layout anticipated for this parcel by the 
Sweetgrass PUD. Adjacent Sweetgrass developments were designed and laid out with this 
development as an integral part of the broader development pattern.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
Staff is satisfied the proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, or 
general welfare.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 

UDC-21-00451  
 
 
March 7, 2022 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff has recommended 
conditions to the plat. These conditions are summarized in the staff recommendation. While this 
condition does not pose a significant engineering or planning issue, it should be addressed prior 
to the Governing Body’s consideration of the Final Plat.  
 
The application complies with conditions for this review criterion. 
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.    
  
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Technical corrections (resolved by map revisions) 
2. Legal description needed to be updated to incorporate the unplatted land to the east 
that is being platted with this plat. (resolved by map revisions) 
3. BOPU notes needed to be corrected. (resolved by map revisions) 

UDC-21-00451  
 
 
March 7, 2022 
CONCLUSION: 
 
This application meets all review criteria as filed. 
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body (Staff Recommendation). 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add conditions, or 
decide contrary to staff’s recommendation.    In the event conditions are modified, the conditions shall relate 
to applicable review criteria.  In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or 
modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for 
approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the Preliminary Plat subject to conditions. Staff has not had significant 
time to review the revised Plats dated 2/28/2022. Conditions may be placed on this approval at the 
Planning Commission meeting should corrections or concerns be found. Staff should note that from a 
quick preliminary review many of Staff’s comments and concerns have been corrected.

3. Master Application Form

5. Final Plat

RESOLUTION NO.   
 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION TO AUTHORIZE THE APPLICATION FOR THE  
SUICIDE INTERVENTION GRANT IN THE AMOUNT OF $73,582.00 
FROM CHEYENNE REGIONAL MEDICAL CENTER FOR THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, OFFICE OF YOUTH ALTERNATIVES”. 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne Office of Youth Alternatives is a not-for-profit 
organization that depends on grants, donations, and local support; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne Office of Youth Alternatives provides suicide 
assessments to youth referred by the community and Laramie County School District #1. 
 
WHEREAS, Memorial Hospital of Laramie County d/b/a Cheyenne Regional Medical 
Center ("CRMC”) operates a nonprofit, tax-exempt, general acute care patient hospital that 
provides inpatient and outpatient services to residents of Laramie County, Wyoming and 
surrounding areas; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives will apply for the 
Suicide Intervention Grant in the amount Seventy-Three Thousand Five Hundred Eighty- Two 
Dollars ($73,582.00) from CRMC amounting to Thirty-Six Thousand Seven Hundred Ninety-
One ($36,791.00) per year for two (2) years. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
1. 
That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in support of the Office of 
Youth Alternatives and the application for the Suicide Intervention Grant from CRMC in 
the amount of Seventy-Three Thousand Five Hundred Eighty- Two Dollars ($73,582.00). 
 
2. 
That the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne is hereby designated as the authorized 
representative of the city to act on behalf of the Governing Body on all matters relating to 
this grant and is authorized to execute the grant agreement and associated documents.

day of 
, 2022.

BOARD OF PUBLIC UTILITIES 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
**SUMMARY OF BOARD MEETING** 
Tuesday, February 24, 2022 
PAGE 1 
 
PRELIMINARY – UNDER IN-HOUSE REVIEW 
The Board of Public Utilities of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming assembled this day 
at 3:00 p.m. in the 1st Floor Conference Room of the Timothy E. Wilson Board of Public 
Utilities’ Administration/Engineering Building, 2416 Snyder Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 
Present via In-Person/Electronic Conference were: 
 
Mary Guthrie, President 
John Edwards, Member 
Keith Zabka, Member 
Allan Cunningham, Member 
Brad Brooks, Director 
Matt Buelow, Water Reclamation Manager 
Doug Calhoun, Operations & Maintenance Manager 
Frank Strong, Eng.& Water Resources Manager 
Clint Bassett, Water Treatment Manager 
Elizabeth Lance, Board Attorney 
Bryce Dorr, Capital Projects Supervisor 
Lynn Butts, Accounting Supervisor 
Sarah Bargsten, Water Conservation Specialist 
Jason Land, Safety & Security Officer 
Erin Lamb, Administrative/Public Affairs 
Pete Laybourn, Cheyenne City Council Member 
Bradley Bowen – Public Attendee 
 
  
 
The Board approved by consent agenda: 
• 
The minutes of the Regular Board Meeting held January 18, 2022. 
• 
Outside User Water and Sewer Services Agreement with Dean Byrne, Lot 3, 
Energy Park One, subject to review and approval by the Governing Body of the 
City of Cheyenne. 
 
 
The Board approved the financial statements for month-end January 31, 2022 as 
presented. The Board approved the vouchers for payment. 
 
 
The Board approved release of retainage to Mechanical Systems Inc. in the amount 
of $44,989.94 for Schedule 2 of the 2020 Sewer Rehabilitation Project, subject to the 
successful completion of W.S. 16-6-116 advertisement period. 
 
 
The Board approved to amend the chemical suppler contract with DPC Industries, 
Inc. to increase the rate for chlorine to $0.5885 per pound. 
 
 
The Board approved reimbursement in the amount of $22,437.77 to WJE, LLC for the 
oversize water main constructed in the Saddle Ridge Subdivision. 
 
 
The Board approved partial release of retainage to RN Civil Construction on the 
amount of $68,023.25 for the Dry Creek Water Reclamation Facility Dewatering 
Improvements Project, subject to the successful completion of W.S. 16-6-116 
advertisement period. 
 
 
The Board approved to award the FY2021 Water Projects to Aztec Construction in 
the amount of $2,479,633.30. 
 
 
The Board approved the agreement with MeGee, Hearne & Paiz LLP to continue to 
provide financial and compliance auditing for the fiscal year ending June 30, 2022 and 
authorize the Director to sign the Professional Services Agreement. Subject to 
Attorney and Governing Body Approval. 
 
 
The Board meeting adjourned at approximately 4:00 p.m.

MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (MOU), by and between the Carbon Asset Network 
(CAN), located at 113 East 20th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and the City of Cheyenne 
(City), a municipal corporation existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming, located at 2101 
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, is entered into and becomes effective on the date of 
the last signature affixed hereto. 
 
WHEREAS, CAN was formed in 2020 as a resource for land managers through the help 
of nonprofits that support soil health, in order to empower the land managers to be a better 
steward of their land.   
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this MOU is to set forth the terms and conditions agreed 
to by the parties regarding the partnership to empower the managers to be a better steward of 
their land.   
 
2. 
Term.  This MOU is effective until terminated by written agreement between the 
parties. 
 
3. 
Process.  CAN recruits land managers into CAN.  
a. 
CAN and land manager will enter into a separate Memorandum of 
Understanding which will require that the land manager will receive funds in exchange 
for working their acreage in a more regenerative and sustainable manner for twelve (12) 
months.   
b. 
CAN’s agronomist will conduct extensive soil testing of the acreage and 
produce a report creating benchmarks for soil organic carbon and other soil elements.  
This sampling is “boots on the ground” sampling and not based on satellite imagery or 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:36 am, Feb 23, 2022

 
Page 2 of 6 
estimations, which are less granular and can only estimate 6 inches below the surface.  
The soil is, instead, sampled at 0 inches to 6 inches and 6 inches to 24 inches for a 
comprehensive test as native plant roots extend to at least 24 inches into the soil profile. 
c. 
The agronomist will send soil samples to a certified lab, upon which their 
analysis will create a report establishing the carbon baseline.  The results of the report 
will then be uploaded onto blockchain.   
d. 
The land manager and CAN’s agronomist will review the results and 
create together a regenerative plan that is customized based on soil content and land 
manager’s goals.  
e. 
The land manager will then execute this plan for twelve (12) months. 
f. 
Sampling will occur again at the conclusion of the first twelve (12) months 
in the same locations as the first samples that were recorded by GPS.  The results will 
then be posted to blockchain and the status of the soil organic carbon is then noted.  
g. 
If the land manager chooses to continue, they meet with agronomist to 
amend the plan and then execute for another twelve (12) months, repeating for as long as 
both parties wish to continue.  
h. 
CAN is responsible for all soil sampling, agronomy consultancy, lab 
analyses, and blockchain operations. 
4. 
Expectations of City of Cheyenne. 
The City will verify that CAN is executing the process outlined above. 
5.  
Expectations of CAN  
CAN will provide the City with the necessary documents to allow the City to verify the 
process outlined in paragraph 4 above.  For serving as verifier of protocol, CAN will pay the 

 
Page 3 of 6 
City two percent (2%) of the granted donations received from the nonprofit.  CAN will complete 
the requirements of the process outlined in paragraph 3 above.  
 
6. 
General Provisions. 
 
 
a. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to 
this MOU which are mutually agreed upon by the parties to this MOU shall be 
incorporated by written instrument, executed and signed by all parties to this MOU. 
b. 
Assignment.  No party may assign or transfer the responsibilities or 
agreements made herein without the prior written consent of the non-assigning parties, 
which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
c. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this 
MOU shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this MOU and the parties, and the venue shall be in 
the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
d. 
Compliance with Laws.  The parties shall keep informed of and comply 
with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations in the performance of this 
MOU. 
  
e. 
Indemnification.  Each party to this MOU assumes the risk of any liability 
arising from its own conduct.  Each party agrees that it will be responsible for any loss, 
claim, damages, or demands resulting from its negligence and the negligence of its 
employees and agents.  Neither party agrees to insure, defend, or indemnify the other. 
f. 
Nondiscrimination.  The parties shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 
1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., the 
Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the 

 
Page 4 of 6 
Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations 
thereto, and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, 
creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy, or qualifying disability 
in connection with the performance under this MOU. 
g. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City does not waive governmental 
immunity by entering into this MOU and specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all 
other applicable laws.  Designation of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and 
similar provisions should not be construed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
h. 
Third-Party Beneficiary Rights.  The parties do not intend to create in any 
other individual or entity, the status of third-party beneficiary and this MOU shall not be 
construed so as to create such status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this 
MOU shall operate only between the parties to this MOU and shall inure solely to the 
benefit of the parties to this MOU. The provisions of this MOU are intended only to 
assist the parties in determining and performing their obligations under this MOU. The 
parties to this MOU intend and expressly agree that only party signatory to this MOU 
shall have any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this MOU, to seek any remedy 
arising out of a party’s performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this 
MOU, or to bring an action for the breach of this MOU. 
i. 
Force Majeure.  The performance of this MOU by the parties shall be 
subject to force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural 
disaster, war or threat of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized 
strikes, governmental regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by 

 
Page 5 of 6 
the World Health Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government 
authority or health agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-
19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other 
similar occurrence beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors, 
circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or 
unreasonably delay the performance required by this MOU.  This MOU may be cancelled 
by any party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or 
amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written 
notice to the other parties. 
j. 
Severability.  If any provision of this MOU is found to be invalid or 
unenforceable for any reason, the remaining provisions will continue to be valid and 
enforceable.  If a court finds that any provision of this MOU is invalid or unenforceable, 
but that by limiting such provision it would become valid and enforceable, then such 
provision will be deemed to be written, construed, and enforced as so limited. 
k. 
Notice.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU 
shall be in writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
l. 
Termination.  Any party may terminate its participation in this MOU by 
providing thirty (30) days written notice to other party. 
m. 
Prior Approval.  This MOU shall not be binding upon the parties, no 
services shall be performed under the terms of this MOU, and no payments shall be made 
until this MOU has been reduced to writing and approved by all necessary authorities. 
n. 
Entirety of Contract.  This MOU represents the entire and integrated 
agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and 

 
Page 6 of 6 
agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
7. 
Signatures.  In witness thereof, the parties to this MOU, through their duly 
authorized representatives, have executed this MOU on the days and dates set out below and 
certify that they have read, understood, and knowingly and voluntarily agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this MOU. 
CAN

CONTRACT NO. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made by and between the THE BOARD OF PUBLIC UTILITIES OF 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, hereinafter referred to as “BOPU,” and POLO RANCH 
COMPANY, hereinafter referred to as “Polo Ranch.” 
 
RECITALS 
 
WHEREAS, Polo Ranch and the City of Cheyenne are parties to a Drilling and Water 
Use Agreement entered into in 1955; and 
 
WHEREAS, over the past several years there have been numerous disputes between the 
parties which have led to litigation and multiple court cases; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Wyoming courts have upheld the validity of the 1955 agreement and the 
City’s interpretation of its meaning; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Polo Ranch now seeks to develop twelve (12) lots, for a total of not 
more than twelve (12) wells, in the Quarter Circle Five subdivision; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has an interest in additional development for the City 
and its residents; and 
 
WHEREAS, the parties wish to enter into this Agreement; 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, for good and valuable consideration, the receipt of which is hereby 
acknowledged by each party, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. BOPU/City agrees to allow Polo Ranch to develop the twelve (12) lots with (12) twelve 
corresponding wells in the Quarter Circle Five subdivision. See attached Exhibit. 
2. This change in use does not affect the validity or enforceability of the City’s water rights 
under the 1955 Agreement and does not increase the City’s obligations under the 1955 
Agreement. 
3. This change in use applies to Polo Ranch’s entire allocation of water per the formula 
under the 1955 Agreement. 
4. BOPU/City shall have no obligation to provide water to or for the Polo Ranch, for any 
use, including water for the lots in Quarter Circle Five, in excess of that determined by 
the formula in the 1955 Agreement. 
5. The deduction from the Polo Ranch’s annual water entitlement for the Quarter Circle 
Five subdivision lots shall remain .7 acre feet per lot. 
6. The Polo Ranch agrees to add deed restrictions to each lot sold to prevent further splitting 
of the twelve (12) lots. 
7. The Polo Ranch agrees to restrict the size of the lawns of the twelve (12) lots within 
Quarter Circle Five subdivision to a maximum of 6,500 square feet. 
By Stefanie Boster at 2:54 pm, Feb 18, 2022

8. Except for the consent of the City as set forth in paragraph 1 above, this Agreement shall 
not affect the rights and duties of the parties under the 1955 Agreement. This Agreement 
shall not be deemed to constitute an amendment of the 1955 Agreement. 
9. In the event any additional documents are necessary to carry out the intent of this 
Agreement, the parties hereto agree to execute and/or obtain such documents promptly 
upon the request of the other party. 
 
DATED this  
day of  
,2022. 
 
 
BOARD OF PUBLIC UTILITIES OF 
POLO RANCH COMPANY 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
 
 
By:  
 
By:  
 
Mary Guthrie, President 
President 
 
 
Attest:  
 
Attest:  
 
Secretary 
Secretary 
 
 
 
Attorney for BOPU 
Attorney for Polo Ranch Company 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE

Patrick Collins, Mayor
