MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
March 7, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement with MHP to Audit Annual Financial 
Statements 
 
 
On March 14, 2022, the Governing Body will be referring to the Finance Committee a 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Board of Public 
Utilities, and McGee, Hearne & Paiz (MHP) LLP to perform the annual fiscal year 2022 audit.  
The cost to the City of Cheyenne for the financial and compliance audit is $71,125 plus $12,875 
to draft the report, including footnote disclosures and related supplementary information, for a 
total of $84,000.  This is an increase of $3,000, or 3.7%, over the fiscal year 2021 audit cost. 
The City Treasurer’s office will be conducting a Request for Proposals for fiscal years 2023-
2025 audit services in December.   
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.  

1 of 9 
 
City Contract # __________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
to 
 
Audit the Annual Financial Statements of the City of Cheyenne  
And its Board of Public Utilities 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _______ day of ________________, 2022, by 
and between the City of Cheyenne (“City”), the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities 
(“Board”), and McGee, Hearne & Paiz LLP (“Firm”). 
 
 
Whereas, the City and the Board require professional services to audit their annual financial 
statements; and 
 
 
Whereas, the Firm is qualified to provide such audits and has previously audited the City 
and Board for the fiscal years ending June 30, 2019, 2020 and 2021; and 
 
 
Whereas, the Firm has submitted a letter dated February 11, 2022 proposing to continue to 
provide financial and compliance auditing services to the City and Board for the fiscal years ending 
June 30, 2022, a copy of which letter is attached to this Agreement as Exhibit A and incorporated 
by this reference; and 
 
 
Whereas, Exhibit A allocates fees for the Firm’s proposed services between the City and 
Board; and 
 
 
Whereas, the Firm also agrees, at the City’s option, to draft the City’s annual financial and 
compliance report for an additional fee to be paid by the City; and 
 
 
Whereas, the City and Board desire to employ the Firm subject to the terms and conditions 
of this Agreement;  
 
Now therefore, in consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, 
it is agreed by and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF SERVICES 
The Firm agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Services attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by 
reference and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
COMPENSATION 
a. In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the City 
and Board agree to pay the Firm the costs set forth in Exhibit A.  The Firm shall bill 
the City and Board separately.  Upon acceptance and approval of the work by the City 
By Stefanie Boster at 3:06 pm, Feb 14, 2022

2 of 9 
 
or the Board, the City and the Board respectively shall make payment therefor within 
45 days of receipt of said invoice.  
 
b. With every periodic invoice, the Firm shall provide the City and the Board, 
respectively, with a written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope 
of Services, time sheets, and work schedule.  Payment shall be based upon actual time 
worked and direct expenses. The Firm shall maintain records supporting time worked 
and direct expenses and shall make those records available to the City or Board at their 
request during normal business hours any time after contract execution and before one 
year after the date of final payment. 
 
c. In the event total fees billed by the Firm for a fiscal year to either the City or the Board 
meet the quoted limits specified in Exhibit A for that fiscal year, and the work required 
of the Firm for that fiscal year has not been completed, the Firm shall remain obligated 
to complete said work in its entirety.  
 
3. 
TERM AND EFFECTIVE DATE 
This Agreement shall commence on the date the last required signature is attached hereto 
and shall continue in full force and effect until the scope of services is completed and any 
documents are submitted to the City or the Board in accordance with Exhibit A, unless this 
Agreement is earlier terminated pursuant to Sections 19 and 20 herein. This Agreement 
may be extended upon agreement of the parties. 
 
4. 
MODIFICATION 
Any changes, modifications, revisions, amendments or extensions to this Agreement which 
are mutually agreed upon by the parties to this Agreement shall be incorporated by written 
instrument, executed and signed by all parties to this Agreement. 
 
5. 
INDEMNIFICATION / HOLD HARMLESS 
The Firm agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City or the Board from and 
against any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and 
expenses incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise 
as a result of death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, 
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental 
laws, regulations or orders to the extent caused by (1) the Firm’s breach of any term or 
provision of this Agreement; or (2) any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by the 
Firm, or its employees or subcontractors in the performance of this Agreement.  The Firm 
acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City or the Board pursuant to 
the terms of this paragraph. 
 
6. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City, the Board, their officials and employees, do not waive their governmental 
immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for the parties to 
pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the Agreement.  
Furthermore, the City and Board specifically retain all immunities and defenses available 
to them as sovereign or governmental entities pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., 

3 of 9 
 
and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity.  
 
7. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Firm shall provide the City and the Board with proof of the following insurance 
coverages: 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or 
destruction of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of 
$1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
Business Automobile Insurance 
Including owned, non-owned and hired vehicles with minimum limits for bodily 
injury and property damage of $1,000,000 per accident for the entire term of the Agreement 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the 
Agreement, as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services 
under this Agreement.  The Firm shall provide the City and the Board with proof of 
workers’ compensation or employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Firm shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City and 
the Board from any and all claims arising from the Firm’s negligence in the performance 
of duties under this Agreement.  The City and the Board prefer that this liability insurance 
coverage be provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
a. The Firm shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, provide the City 
and the Board with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is 
or shall be in effect at the time the Firm begins the provision of services under this 
Agreement; and 
b. In the event the Firm’s services extend into a future policy period, the Firm shall, 
prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City and the Board with a new 
certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect 
during all periods of time that the Firm will provide services under this Agreement; 
and 
c. The Firm shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five (5) years 
following the last date that the Firm has provided services under this Agreement; 
and 

4 of 9 
 
d. In the event the Firm or the insurer terminates “claims made” coverage prior to the 
expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., b., or c. of this paragraph, 
the Firm shall provide to the City and the Board advance written notification of the 
termination of said coverage and shall provide the City and the Board with an 
endorsement for an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) which shall be in 
effect for a period of time not less than five (5) years following the last date that 
the Firm has provided services under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
The Firm shall name the City and the Board as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City and the Board with a copy 
of the endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
The Firm shall provide the City and the Board with certificates of insurance 
acknowledging the above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this 
Agreement. 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  
It shall be an affirmative obligation upon the Firm to immediately notify in writing the City 
and the Board of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has resulted in or may result in 
the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance coverage required by this 
Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
If requested by the City or the Board, the Firm shall provide the City or Board, 
respectively, with copies of insurance policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City 
and the Board as an additional insured.  The City’s of Board’s failure to request or review 
such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not affect the City’s or Board’s rights or 
the Firm’s obligation hereunder. 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a 
minimum A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
8. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
9. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
10. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Firm shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment 
Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), 
as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any 
properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, and shall not discriminate against any 

5 of 9 
 
individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, 
pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection with the performance under this 
Agreement. 
 
11. 
ALCOHOL AND DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug Free Work Place Act of November 1988, the City and Board 
have established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to alcohol and 
drug usage by City and Board employees. All parties under contract with City or Board, 
and their employees and subcontractors, are required to comply with the provisions of the 
City’s and Board’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage 
on City or Board property or other sites occupied by the Firm while performing the duties 
and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Firm to familiarize 
themselves with the requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and 
subcontractors of the requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Firm, their 
employees or subcontractors are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be 
terminated. 
 
12. 
INDEPENDENT CONTRACTOR 
The Firm shall function as an independent contractor for the purposes of this Agreement. 
The Firm shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that may be incurred 
by the Firm in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this Agreement shall be 
interpreted as authorizing the Firm or its agents or employees to act as an agent or 
representative of or on behalf of the City or the Board or to incur any obligation of any 
kind on the behalf of the City or the Board. 
 
13. 
CONFIDENTIALTY 
To the extent allowed by law, the City, the Board, and the Firm shall treat as confidential 
and not disclose to others information (including technical information, experience or data) 
regarding either party's plans, programs, plants, processes, products, costs, equipment, 
operations, or customers which come within the knowledge of the parties, without in each 
instance securing the prior written consent of the other party, unless such disclosure is 
required by law or legal process. However, nothing shall prevent either the Firm, the City, 
or the Board from disclosing to others or using in any manner information which either 
party can show (a) has been published or has become part of the public domain other than 
by acts of the parties; (b) has been furnished or made known to the parties by third parties 
without restrictions on its disclosure; or (c) was in either party's possession prior to the 
disclosure thereof by the parties to each other. No party shall be restricted from releasing 
information in response to a subpoena, court order, or legal process, but, in the event such 
disclosure becomes necessary, the disclosing party shall notify the other party of the 
demand for information at least seven (7) days prior to disclosing such information so that 
the other party may take any and all steps provided by law to prevent the release of such 
Information. 
 
14. 
FIRM’S RESPONSIBILITIES 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Firm shall perform in a manner consistent 
with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same profession 

6 of 9 
 
currently practicing under similar circumstances at the same time and in the same or similar 
locality. Upon notice to the Firm and by mutual agreement between the parties, the Firm 
will, without additional compensation, correct those services not meeting such a standard. 
 
15. 
NOTICES 
Any notice, correspondence or billing required to be given by the terms of this Agreement 
shall be delivered by hand, or delivered by mail, postage prepaid, to the addresses of the 
respective parties stated below: 
 
CITY:  
Robin Lockman, CPA, City Treasurer 
 
 
2101 O'Neil Avenue 
Cheyenne. WY 82001 
 
 
BOARD: 
Brad Brooks, Director 
2416 Snyder Avenue 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
 
FIRM:  
Stephanie Pickering, CPA, Partner 
314 West 18111 Street 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
16. 
CONFLICT OF INTEREST  
In entering this Agreement, the Firm covenants that it presently has no interest, and shall 
not acquire any interest, direct or indirect, financial or otherwise, which would conflict in 
any manner or degree with performance of the services hereunder. In addition. the Firm 
covenants that in the performance of this contract, no subcontractor or person having such 
an interest shall be employed. The Firm certifies that no one who has or will have any 
financial interest under this contract is an officer or employee of the City or the Board. 
 
17. 
ACCEPTANCE NOT WAIVER 
The City's or the Board's approval of reports, and incidental work or materials furnished 
hereunder shall not in any way relieve the Firm from responsibility for the technical 
accuracy of the work. The City's or the Board's approval or acceptance of, or payment for, 
any services shall not be construed to operate as a waiver of any of the City's or the Board's 
rights under this Agreement or any of its legal rights under statute and common law arising 
out of the performance of this Agreement. 
 
18. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
19. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party declares the other party in default hereof, said party declaring default 
shall notify the defaulting party in writing and such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days to cure said default. In the event that the default remains 

7 of 9 
 
uncorrected, the party not in default may elect to: 
a. Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not exceed the 
contract amount;  
b. Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance; 
c. Avail itself of any other remedy at law or equity 
In the event the Firm fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the City 
or the Board may elect to make good such deficiencies and charge the Firm therefore. 
 
20. 
TERMINATION 
The City or the Board may, by written notice to the Firm, terminate this Agreement in 
whole or in part by giving the Firm fifteen (15) days written notice. Upon receipt of such 
notice, the Firm shall: 
a. Discontinue all services affected (unless the notice directs otherwise); and 
b. Deliver to the City or the Board respectively within five (5) days all data, 
documents, reports, estimates, or summaries, accumulated by the Firm in 
performing this Agreement, whether completed or in process. In event of 
termination, the City or the Board will pay the Firm for accepted work done as of 
the date of termination. 
 
21. 
WAIVER 
The waiver by either party of any term, condition or covenant, or breach of any term, 
condition or covenant, shall not constitute a waiver of any other term, condition or 
covenant, or breach thereof. 
 
22. 
SEVERABILITY  
If any provision, section, subsection, sentence, clause, or phrase of this Agreement is 
invalidated by any court of competent jurisdiction, such holding shall not affect the validity 
of the remainder of this Agreement, which shall continue in full force and affect. 
 
23. 
SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS 
All the terms, conditions, and provisions herein shall inure to the benefit of and be binding 
upon the parties hereto and their respective successors and assigns. 
 
24. 
ASSIGNMENT 
No party shall assign this Agreement without prior written consent of both parties. Any 
delegation or assignment shall not operate to relieve either party of its responsibilities 
hereunder. However, nothing in this Agreement shall prohibit the Firm from subcontracting 
to a qualified subcontractor. 
 
25. 
THIRD PARTY RIGHTS 
The parties do not intend to create in any other individual or entity the status of third-party 
beneficiary, and this Agreement shall not be construed so as to create such status. The 
rights, duties and obligations contained in this Agreement shall operate only between the 
parties to this Agreement and shall inure solely to the benefit of the parties to this 
Agreement. The parties to this Agreement intend and expressly agree that only the parties 
signatory to this Agreement shall have any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this 

8 of 9 
 
Agreement, to seek any remedy arising out of a party's performance or failure to perform 
any term or condition of this Agreement, or to bring action for breach of this Agreement. 
This paragraph is not intended nor shall it be construed to waive any of the parties' 
immunities. 
 
26. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement, to include Exhibit A, contains the entire understanding of the parties. 
There are no other terms or conditions, written or oral, concerning or controlling this 
matter.  
 
 
 
 
[REST OF THIS PAGE INTENTIONALLY LEFT BLANK] 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

9 of 9

representatives, have executed this Agreement on the days and dates set out below and certify that 
they have read, understood and agreed to the terms and conditions of this Agreement

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_83019935.pdf
 
Page 1 of 9 
CITY CONTRACT NO. ________ 
 
ADULT SOFTBALL OFFICIATING AGREEMENT 
 
 
This Agreement is made and entered into this _______ day of ________________, 2022, 
by and between the City of Cheyenne, a municipal corporation of the State of Wyoming, 2101 
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, hereinafter referred to as “City,” and the Cheyenne 
Umpires Association, 1505 East 16th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, hereinafter referred to 
as “Association.” 
W I T N E S SE T H: 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants and conditions contained 
herein, it is hereby agreed as follows: 
1. 
Leagues and Role.  Association will umpire and score games and tournaments during 
the City’s 2022 season for Adult Men’s and Women’s Softball Leagues, Co-Rec Softball 
League, and tournaments scheduled by the City of Cheyenne Recreation Division. 
2. 
Game Fee to Umpires and Scorekeepers.  City shall pay umpires Thirty-Five Dollars 
($35.00) per Adult Men’s and Women’s Softball games, Adult Co-Rec Softball games, 
and Tournament games (one umpire required), and Fifteen Dollars ($15.00) per game to 
the scorekeeper (one scorekeeper required).  Umpires in Training will receive Fifteen 
Dollars ($15.00) per game up to ten (10) games total. Refer to 4. Payment for tracking 
and payment of Umpires in Training. 
3. 
Additional Fees.  Association will charge City a one-time $600 annual administrative 
service fee for the three leagues (Men’s Softball League, Women’s Softball League, and 
Co-Rec Softball League) and tournaments.  In addition, a $.35 (thirty-five cents) per 
scheduled game fee will be charged to the City.   
By Stefanie Boster at 9:05 am, Mar 09, 2022

 
Page 2 of 9 
4. 
Payment.   
a. Association will invoice the City for the first 400 games.  Upon receipt of the invoice, 
City will process immediately through the City Treasurer’s Office payable system.  The 
City processes invoices on a schedule with payable cut-off dates, submits to City Council 
approval process, and then checks are cut and mailed. Association may turn in the first 
400 games invoice immediately following approval of this Agreement. 
b. Association will invoice the City for the subsequent games to be umpired by the 
Association for the balance of the initial league as scheduled by the City shall be 
submitted prior to the mid-point of the season. 
c. Within thirty (30) days after the conclusion of each league, the City will invoice 
Association for all fines accumulated and credit any games not paid for.  Association 
shall pay invoice within 45 days of receipt of the invoice. 
d. Association will invoice City immediately following a tournament, and the City will 
process the invoice through the normal City Treasurer’s Office payables system. 
e. Association will invoice City for Umpires in Training separately.  Invoice will include 
Umpire in Training names, dates, and number of games officiated.  
5. 
Assignment of Umpires and Scorekeepers.  Association shall provide and assign one 
(1) umpire and one (1) scorekeeper for the Adult Men’s and Women’s League, the Co-
Rec Softball League, and City tournaments to umpire and score games as referred to 
above according to the schedule provided to the Association by the City.   
6. 
Schedule.  Association shall provide the City with a schedule of the umpires and 
scorekeepers assigned to each game at least two (2) weeks in advance of each game. 
7. 
Contacts.  Association shall provide the City with a list of names and telephone 

 
Page 3 of 9 
numbers of all Association personnel and officials at least one (1) week before the first 
game of a tournament or a league game.  The Association will update the list as changes 
occur and provide a copy to the City. 
8. 
Training.  Association shall ensure that all umpires assigned to the games are properly 
trained and certified to perform their duties in accordance with the current year’s 
Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events, Recreation Division’s By-Laws, League 
Rules, and National Association Code and Rule Book.  The City Recreation Division 
will provide to Association Bylaws and League Rules two (2) months prior to the first 
game. 
a. Any umpire or scorekeeper deemed by the City not to be properly trained or able to 
perform their duties can be prohibited by the City to umpire or score. 
9. 
Uniform. Umpires are required to wear a uniform designated by the Association. 
a. There will be a Five Dollars ($5.00) fine for not being properly dressed as stated in 
this Agreement. 
10. 
Ejections.  Association must report all player ejections to the site supervisor on the same 
day of the game by completing the required ejection form.   
11. 
Inclement Weather.  Parties mutually agree that if inclement weather begins after the 
umpires and scorekeeper have arrived for the first scheduled game and the game is 
cancelled before being started, the umpires and scorekeepers will receive travel pay in the 
amount of $5.00 each.  The umpires and scorekeeper must sign in. 
a. The parties further agree the maximum delay due to inclement weather will be thirty 
(30) minutes.   
b. If games are cancelled due to inclement weather, all subsequent games are cancelled. 

 
Page 4 of 9 
c. It is further agreed that on weekends (tournaments only), the decision to cancel one or 
more games at a given field will not prohibit games on that field from resuming at a 
time specified by the City. 
d. The parties mutually agree if a game is cancelled due to weather after being started 
and within 20 minutes into the game, the Association will be paid half the current 
game fee $17.50 for umpires, $7.50 for Umpires in Training, and $7.50 for 
scorekeepers.  If a game is cancelled due to weather after being started and 21 
minutes into the game, Association will be paid the full game fee. 
12. 
Cancellations.  City will notify Association of any cancellations or forfeitures by 3:30 
p.m. on the day of the scheduled game. If notification is made after 3:30 p.m., City will 
pay the Association the game fee to the umpire(s) and scorekeeper(s).  If a game is 
cancelled or forfeited and the Association was notified by 3:30 p.m., the Association will 
not be paid for the game if it is a first or last game.   
13. 
Reporting into Site Supervisor.  The Association hereby agrees that should it fail to 
provide the proper number of umpires and scorekeepers in a timely manner as required 
by this Agreement it shall pay the City fines for each failure as per the following: 
a. Association shall ensure that umpires and scorekeepers sign-in with the site 
supervisor at least ten (10) minutes prior to the scheduled game time.  A sign-in 
sheet will be provided to the site supervisor by the City.   
i. In the event an umpire/scorekeeper is late signing in with the Site Supervisor, 
the Association will be fined Five Dollars ($5.00) each. 
b. 
If umpires/scorekeepers fail to sign in prior to scheduled game time: 
i. 
One half the current umpire fee per umpire will be fined. 

 
Page 5 of 9 
ii. 
One half the current scorekeeper fee per scorekeeper will be fined. 
14. 
Scoring.  Association scorekeeper shall keep and maintain a score book, score report 
form, and distribute lineup cards for each game.  The scorekeeper shall mark the official 
starting time for each game in the scorebook and enter the first initial and last names of 
each player into the score book. The scorekeeper shall also fill out the score report form 
and have the umpire sign score book after each game to verify the score of each game 
and then the scorekeeper shall turn in the score report form to the site supervisor. 
a. Failure to turn in the score report form, the Association will be fined Ten 
Dollars ($10.00) per game 
b. Failure to report a score correctly, Association will be fined Ten Dollars 
($10.00) per game. 
c. Sanctioned League.  City shall provide a National Association sanctioned league 
and maintain all requirements for sanctioning. 
15. 
Independent Contractor.  At all times during the term of this Agreement, the 
Association shall be considered an independent contractor. Neither the Association nor 
anyone employed by it shall represent, act, purport to act, or be deemed to be the agent, 
representative, employee, or servant of the City. 
16. 
Acceptance Not Waiver.  The City’s approval of, acceptance of, or payment for any of 
the services of this Agreement shall not be construed to operate as a waiver of any of the 
City’s rights under this Agreement or any of its legal rights under statute or common law 
arising out of the performance of this Agreement. 
17. 
Default.  Every term and condition contained in this Agreement, including payment 
obligations, shall be deemed a material element of this Agreement.  In the event either 

 
Page 6 of 9 
party should fail or refuse to perform according to the terms of the Agreement, such party 
may be declared in default. 
18. 
Remedies. 
a.  
In the event that the City declares the Association in default of this Agreement, 
the City shall notify the Association in writing.  The Association shall be allowed a 
period of thirty (30) days to cure the default.  In the event the default remains 
uncorrected, the Association may elect to: 
 
 
i. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, 
 
 
ii. 
treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance; or, 
 
 
iii. 
avail itself of any other remedy at law or equity. 
19. 
Termination.  The City representative may, by thirty (30) days written notice to the 
Association, terminate this Agreement in whole or in part for the City’s convenience.  In 
the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Association for work 
performed as of the date of termination. 
20. 
Notices.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this Agreement shall be in 
writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person as follows: 
 
 
Cheyenne Umpires Association 
 
City of Cheyenne, CRE 
 
Attn: Todd Strickler  
 
 
Recreation Division 
 
1505 East 16th Street  
 
 
1317 Parsley Blvd. 
 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001  
 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82007 
 
21. 
Waiver.  The waiver by either party of any term, condition, covenant, or breach of any 
term, condition or covenant shall not constitute a waiver of any other term, condition, or 
covenant or the breach thereof. 

 
Page 7 of 9 
22. 
Governing Law, Jurisdiction, and Venue.  The construction, interpretation, and 
enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  
The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over any action arising out of 
this Agreement and the parties, and the venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie 
County, Wyoming. 
23. 
Compliance with Laws.  The parties agree that they will perform their obligations as 
provided in this Agreement in accordance with the applicable laws and ordinances. 
24. 
Severability.  If any provision, section, subsection, sentence, clause, or phrase of this 
Agreement is invalidated by any court of competent jurisdiction, such holding will not 
affect the validity of the remainder of the Agreement, which shall continue in full force 
and effect. 
25. 
Successors and Assigns.  All the terms, conditions, and provisions herein shall inure to 
the benefit of and be binding upon the parties hereto and their respective successors and 
assigns. 
26. 
Assignment.  Neither party shall assign this Agreement and any attempt to assign shall 
be void without prior written consent of both parties.  Any delegation or assignment 
shall not operate to relieve either party of its responsibilities under this Agreement.  
However, nothing in this Agreement shall prohibit the Association from subcontracting to 
a qualified subcontractor with written consent of the City. 
27. 
Drug-Free Workplace.  In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of 
November 1988, the City has established an alcohol and controlled substance policy that 
pertains alcohol and drug usage by City employees.  All independent contractors under 
contract with the City, and their employees or subcontractors, are required to comply 

 
Page 8 of 9 
with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy for drug 
and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites occupied by the Association while 
performing the duties and responsibilities of this Agreement.  It is the responsibility of 
the Association to familiarize itself with the requirements of this policy and to inform all 
its employees and subcontractors of the requirements and to insure compliance with it.  
If the Association, its employees, or subcontractors are found in violation of this policy, 
this Agreement may be terminated. 
28. 
Minority Business Enterprises.  All parties to this Agreement assure that no person 
shall be excluded from participation in, denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated 
against, in connection with the award and performance of this Agreement on the grounds 
of age, race, color, disability, national origin, or sex.  The parties further assure that it 
will include the language of this paragraph in all agreements associated or connected in 
any way with this Agreement and shall cause all existing Agreements to similarly include 
this clause therein. 
29. 
EEOC and ADA Compliance.  The Association is notified that it must comply fully 
with all requirements of the Equal Employment Opportunity Commission (EEOC) and 
the Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA) in the same manner as is expected from the 
City.  If the Association or its employees or subcontractors are found in violation of 
these requirements, this Agreement may be terminated.  The Association shall be 
responsible for all non-compliant action and shall defend, hold harmless, and indemnify 
the City from such action. 
30. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City reserves all immunities and defenses available 
under the Wyoming Governmental Claims Act, Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq. 

 
Page 9 of 9 
31. 
Term.  This Agreement shall commence on the _____ day of ______, 2022 and 
conclude on the last day of the 2022 Adult Co-Rec Softball season. 
32. 
Entire Agreement of Parties.  This Agreement contains the entire understanding of the 
parties.  There are no other terms or conditions, written or oral, concerning or controlling 
this matter.  Time is of the essence. 
 
In witness thereof, the parties to this Agreement, through their duly authorized 
representatives, have executed this Agreement on the days and dates set out above and certify 
that they have read, understood, and agreed to the terms and conditions of this Agreement.

1 
 
SECTION 1.  PARTIES 
 
The parties to this Agreement are the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a municipal 
corporation, hereinafter referred to as the “City,” and the Cheyenne Firefighters, Local Union 
No. 279, International Association of Firefighters, hereinafter referred to as the “Union.” 
 

2 
 
SECTION 2.  DEFINITIONS 
Acceptable Fire-Training Academy: 
A professionally certified school whose program is the equivalent of the academy provided by 
the Front Range Fire Consortium. 
Bargaining Agent: 
Local Union No. 279 is the agent representing the firefighters for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and shall be recognized as the sole and exclusive bargaining agent and shall have the 
scope of authority to bargain for rates of pay, hours of work, and other working conditions. 
Base Pay: 
The agreed rate of pay for a firefighter based on his or her rank and step as specified in Section 
21 excluding overtime pay, longevity pay, holiday pay, education pay, and specialty pay. 
Cadre/Training Instructor: 
The Cadre/Training Instructor position will be utilized to fulfill Cheyenne Fire and Rescue’s 
obligation to the Front Range Fire Consortium, its need for qualified instructors and to offset 
associated costs with participation.  It may also be used to meet the training needs of the 
Department outside of academy opportunities.  Assignments made to this position shall not 
exceed twelve months in duration.  If a Member is retained for time periods outside of an active 
academy, the Member shall be utilized to meet the training demands of Cheyenne Fire Rescue.  
An assignment made to fill this position shall not violate Section 15. 
Civil Service Commission: 
The City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission. 
 

3 
 
Command Officers: 
The Fire Chief, Division Chiefs, and Battalion Chiefs.  
Company Officers: 
Lieutenants. 
Day Personnel: 
A Member who works an eight or ten-hour tour of duty. 
Days: 
For any period of time specified in this Agreement, days shall be computed in accordance with 
the Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission. 
Department: 
The Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department. 
Eligibility List: 
A list established by the City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission pursuant 
to its Rules and Regulations.  
E.M.S.: 
Shall mean Emergency Medical Services. 
E.O.D.: 
Shall mean earned off-duty time or compensatory time off. 
 
Extraordinary Circumstances: 
An event or condition which could not reasonably be anticipated or expected to occur and is 
beyond the usual, customary, or regular duties of firefighters. 
 

4 
 
Fire Chief: 
The Chief of the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department. 
Grievance: 
The procedure for the resolution of disputes arising under this Agreement as specified in Section 
8. 
Immediate Family: 
 
 
Spouse

Daughter 
 
 
Grandfather 
 
Son 
 
 
 
Grandchildren 
 
Mother 
 
 
Mother-in-Law 
 
Father  
 
 
Father-in-Law 
 
Sister  
 
 
Sister-in-Law 
 
Brother 
 
 
Brother-in-Law 
 
Step-Mother  
 
Daughter-in-Law 
 
Step-Father 
 
 
Son-in-Law 
 
Step-Children 
 
Incident Commander: 
The highest ranking Officer who initially responds to any call for service or who has accepted 
transfer of command. 
Members: 
All regularly sworn employees of the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department, as covered by the 
Collective Labor Agreement (excluding the Fire Chief). 
Officer in Charge: 
Any Officer in a Member’s chain of command. 
On-Call: 
Members may be required to be available twenty-four hours per day for seven consecutive days 
in a month to report to work for the performance of work relating to the duties and 

5 
 
responsibilities of the Prevention Division. 
Platoon Personnel: 
A Member who works a twenty-four-hour tour of duty. 
Rank: 
Any of the ranks specified in the Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne Fire Department 
Civil Service Commission. 
Rules and Regulations: 
The Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission. 
Seniority: 
The time of consecutive service a Member has accumulated from the date of the Member’s 
employment and shall not be interpreted as time in grade or rank. 
“Shall” and “May”: 
“Shall” is mandatory; “May” is permissive. 
Shift: 
The number of hours worked in a tour of duty by: 
 
A. 
Day Personnel - Personnel assigned to work a forty-hour week (Monday through 
Friday). 
 
B. 
Platoon Personnel - Personnel assigned to work twenty-four-hour shifts. 
Shift Trainers 
Personnel who have been assigned to assist Fire Training or EMS.  
Specialty Team 
Cheyenne Fire Rescue personnel assigned to the Hazardous Materials and Specialized Rescue 

6 
 
Teams.   
Temporary Assignment: 
An assignment to a position and rank for a period of no more than twelve months to meet the 
needs of the Department.  The Department shall not assign more than one Member at any given 
time. 
Vacancy: 
A vacancy is any open position resulting from resignation, termination, retirement, death, 
promotion, or voluntary or involuntary demotion. 
 

7 
 
SECTION 3.  EXCLUSIVE BARGAINING AGENT 
 
The paid Members of the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department, the regularly constituted 
Fire Department of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, by a majority vote, have selected the 
I.A.F.F. Local Union No. 279 as the sole and exclusive bargaining agent for all of the Members 
of the Department, except the Fire Chief. 
 
The Union and the City’s obligation to negotiate is subject to Wyo. Stat. §§ 27-10-101 
through 27-10-109. 
 
The City shall not enter into any other agreement with Members serving as firefighters, 
individually or collectively, or with any other organization, which in any way conflicts with the 
provisions of this Collective Labor Agreement. 
 

8 
 
SECTION 4.  TERM OF AGREEMENT 
 
This Agreement shall become effective 12:00 a.m., July 1, 2022, and remain in effect 
until 11:59 p.m., June 30, 2023. 

9 
 
SECTION 5.  SEPARABILITY 
 
If any clause, sentence, paragraph, or part of this Agreement, or the application thereof to 
any person or circumstance, shall for any reason be adjudged by a court of competent jurisdiction 
to be invalid, such judgment shall not affect, impair, or invalidate the remainder of the 
Agreement. 

10 
 
SECTION 6.  EMBODIMENT OF AGREEMENT 
 
This Collective Labor Agreement constitutes the sole and complete agreement between 
the parties and embodies all the terms and conditions governing the wages, rates of pay, and 
working conditions of the Members represented by the Union.   All costs associated with the 
arbitration shall be shared equally by the City and the Union.  The parties acknowledge they 
have had the opportunity to present and discuss any subject germane to collective bargaining.  
Any prior commitment or agreement between the City and the Union or any individual Member 
covered by this Agreement that has been negotiated by the Union is hereby superseded. 
 

11 
 
SECTION 7.  RE-NEGOTIATIONS 
 
At any time prior to the expiration date of this Agreement, any provision herein may be 
re-negotiated by mutual agreement of the City and the Union.  No part of this Agreement shall be 
opened until mutual agreement is reduced in writing and signed by both parties, consisting of the 
bargaining committee for the Union and the Governing Body of the City.  Only the issue(s) in 
question shall be re-negotiated, and not the entire Agreement. 
 

12 
 
SECTION 8.  GRIEVANCE PROCEDURES 
 
A grievance is defined as a dispute or difference of opinion between the City and a 
Member, Members, or the Union over the meaning, interpretation, application, or the inequitable 
application of an expressed provision of the Agreement.  Any Member may file a grievance 
under this Agreement protesting the exercise of Management Rights, but to prevail, the Member 
must establish a violation by the City of the specific terms of this Agreement.  Any grievance 
filed against the City shall be in writing and if a Member’s grievance, it shall contain the name 
and position of the affected Member(s).  The grievance shall contain a clear and concise 
statement of the grievance, the issue or issues involved, the relief sought, the date the incident or 
violation took place, and the specific section or sections alleged to have been violated. 
 
For a grievance to be considered timely, the Union’s Grievance Committee must submit 
it in writing within ten business days of the date the grievant knew of the event giving rise to the 
dispute, following the steps below: 
Step 1 
 
The Union’s Grievance Committee may submit the grievance in writing to the respective 
Division Chief who will then schedule a meeting with the grievant and two members of the 
Union’s Grievance Committee within ten business days to discuss the grievance and attempt to 
resolve the grievance.  A written decision from the Division Chief shall be delivered to the 
grievant, Union, and Fire Chief within five business days of the meeting.  If the decision fails to 
satisfy the grievant and/or Union, either may initiate Step 2 of the grievance process. 
 
Step 2 

13 
 
 
A grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee who does not agree with the Step 1 
response and still feels that they have been grieved, may initiate Step 2 and forward the original 
grievance and written responses from Step 1 to the Fire Chief within ten business days of receipt 
of the written decision from Step 1. 
 
Upon receipt, the Fire Chief shall have five business days to schedule and meet with 
grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee to hear the grievance and associated facts to seek 
a resolution.  If a resolution is reached, the decision shall be reduced to writing and signed by all 
parties within ten business days.  If a resolution cannot be reached, the Fire Chief shall deliver a 
decision to the parties involved and Mayor within ten business days.  If the decision fails to 
satisfy the grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee, either may initiate Step 3 of the 
process. 
 
It is understood that nothing shall preclude the Grievance Committee from electing to 
skip Step 1 and begin with presenting a grievance to the Fire Chief if it is deemed that such 
action is warranted by the nature or circumstances of the grievance. 
Step 3 
 
A grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee who does not agree to the Step 2 
response and still feels they have been grieved, may initiate Step 3 and forward all original 
grievance and responses to the Mayor within ten business days of receipt of the Fire Chief’s 
decision. The Mayor and Fire Chief shall meet with all parties involved within ten business days 
of receipt of the Step 3 notification. 
 
In attendance at the meeting shall be the Mayor or his or her designee, the aggrieved 
party, the Union’s Grievance Committee, and at the Union’s option, its legal counsel.  If the 

14 
 
grievance is settled as a result of such meeting, the settlement shall be reduced to writing and 
signed by the parties within five days following the meeting.  If no settlement is reached, the 
Mayor or his or her designee shall give a written answer to the grievance within five business 
days following the meeting.  The grievance may then be submitted to arbitration by the Union. 
Arbitration 
 
Within five business days from the receipt of the Mayor or his or her designee’s written 
answer referred to above, the City and the Union shall request in writing that the American 
Arbitration Association shall submit a list of five arbitrators.  Within five business days after 
receipt of said list, one impartial arbitrator shall be selected.  A coin flip shall determine which 
side will be first in striking a name from the list.  The name last remaining shall be the appointed 
impartial arbitrator. 
 
Arbitration shall proceed pursuant to the provisions of the Uniform Arbitration Act.  
(Wyo. Stat. §§ 1-36-101 through 1-36-119).  The selected arbitrator shall have no right to amend, 
modify, alter, add to, or subtract from the provisions of the Agreement.  The arbitrator shall only 
consider and make a decision with respect to the specific issue or issues submitted and shall have 
no authority to make a decision on any other issue not so submitted to the arbitrator.  In the event 
the arbitrator finds a violation of the terms of the Agreement, the arbitrator shall fashion an 
appropriate remedy.  Any dispute, claim, or grievance arising from or relating to interpretation or 
application of this Agreement shall be submitted to arbitration administered by the American 
Arbitration Association under its Labor Arbitration Rules, unless the parties otherwise have 
agreed in this Section.  The parties further agree to accept the arbitrator’s award as final and 
binding on them, subject to the right of either party to seek judicial review pursuant to Wyo. Stat. 

15 
 
§§ 1-36-101, et seq.  The parties shall each pay half the costs and expenses of the impartial 
arbitrator.  Should the same issue be submitted to arbitration two or more times during the term 
of this Agreement and the same party loses two or more times on that issue, the losing party shall 
pay all costs and expenses of the second or any subsequent impartial arbitration. 

16 
 
SECTION 9.  NON-DISCRIMINATION 
 
Neither the City nor the Union shall discriminate against a Member of the Department by 
any means whatsoever because of race, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, creed, color, 
national origin, or Union affiliation with I.A.F.F. Local Union No. 279.  The City and the Union 
shall comply with the requirements of Title VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, as amended, the 
Fair Employment Practices Act of 1965, as amended (Wyo. Stat. §§ 27-9-101, et seq.), and other 
applicable state and federal laws. 
 

17 
 
SECTION 10.  ANNEXATIONS 
 
The City and the Union agree that should the City annex any new areas the Department 
will provide first response to all calls in all annexed areas with the exception of the F. E. Warren 
Air Force Base. 
 

18 
 
SECTION 11.  VOLUNTARY DEMOTION 
 
Any Member who voluntarily requests a demotion from the Member’s current rank shall 
be demoted to the rank held immediately prior to the Member’s promotion.  The Member shall 
make the request in writing at least thirty days prior to the effective date of the requested 
demotion.  The request shall be subject to approval by the Fire Chief.  The Fire Chief may not, 
prior to the expiration of the thirty-day period, appoint another Member to the position from 
which the Member wishes to be demoted.  Upon the effective date, the Member shall return to 
their previous rank and grade. 
 

19 
 
SECTION 12.  UNION REPRESENTATION 
 
In the event any Member of the Department appears before the Fire Chief, the Mayor or 
his or her designee, for work-related disciplinary action, that Member shall have the option of 
having one elected Union representative present.  The person or persons being disciplined shall 
only be called in while on duty.  The elected Union representative shall be granted time off 
without loss of wages. 
 
For the purpose of this Section, the term “work-related disciplinary action” includes, but 
is not limited to, verbal or written reprimands, demotion, suspension, or termination actions that 
become part of the Member’s personnel file. 
 

20 
 
SECTION 13.  MANAGEMENT RIGHTS 
 
Except as otherwise provided in this Agreement, the City shall have the right to direct 
and manage the operations of the Department and its employees, and to maintain the efficiency 
of the Department including: 
 
1. 
The determination of Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department policy, including the 
right to manage the affairs of the Department. 
 
2. 
The right to establish and modify the amount of apparatus in the main or reserve 
fleet. 
 
3. 
The right to direct the members of the Department, including the right to hire, 
promote, discipline, or transfer Members to another shift. 
 
4. 
The collaboration for the safety, health, and property-protection measurers 
provided by the Department for the citizens of Cheyenne. 
 
5. 
The selection and promotion of employees established by the Civil Service 
Commission in its rules and regulations. 
 
6. 
The determination of policy training of employees. 
 
7. 
The establishment, modification, and enforcement of Department rules, 
regulations, and orders. 
 
8. 
The determination of the amount of supervision necessary. 
 
9. 
The transfer of employees from one station to the other. 
 
10. 
The establishment and determination of specialized emergency response teams to 
include roles, responsibilities, and training requirements. 
 
11. 
The right to establish job descriptions for each duty position within the 

21 
 
department as identified in Section 21, Classification of Employment and Basic Wages.  
 
It is agreed that no conduct or action of the City or Union hereunder shall breach the 
provisions of the Agreement, the Civil Service Rules and Regulations in effect on July 1, 2021, 
and the laws of the State of Wyoming governing Firefighters, and the discretionary power 
invested in the City and the Fire Chief shall not be exercised in an arbitrary or capricious 
manner. 

22 
 
SECTION 14.  STRIKES 
 
During the term of this Agreement, there shall be no strike, slowdown, suspension, or 
work stoppage by the Members of the Department, nor shall there be any lock-out or work 
interference by the City. 
 

23 
 
SECTION 15.  DEPARTMENT STAFFING 
 
The Department shall maintain a minimum staffing level of ninety-one firefighters, 
including the Fire Chief, with twenty-seven firefighters assigned to each shift. When Department 
staffing falls below the minimum level of firefighters, it shall be brought back to the minimum 
level as follows: 
 
The Fire Chief shall utilize the current Civil Service Commission eligibility list to begin 
the replacement process for the available openings.  The Fire Chief may elect to: 
1. 
Hire person(s) who have two or more years of documented paid 
professional firefighting experience, with initial training equal to or higher than the Front 
Range Fire Academy to the level of Firefighter 1 and Firefighter 2.  Additionally, the 
potential new hire must submit all continuing education completed by them as 
documented by their current training division for review.  If the potential new hire meets 
or exceeds the base minimum training as compared to Department standards, the Chief 
may elect to hire the person(s) and have them begin an orientation program and upon 
completion report to shift work; or 
2. 
Hire personnel and send them through the Front Range Fire Academy or 
equivalent program for new firefighter training the to acceptable levels of Firefighter 1 
and Firefighter 2 certification. 
 
To accommodate fire-training academy schedules, the Department may delay hiring new 
Members until seven days prior to the start of spring and fall academies, while still recognizing 
the importance of minimum staffing levels. 
 

24 
 
 
A. 
If a Member separates from service within forty-five days or less of the start of a 
fire training academy program, the City is not required to hire a replacement to attend the 
immediate academy but shall have hired the replacement(s) at least seven days prior to the 
subsequent academy. 
 
B. 
The staffing of the Operations, Prevention, and Training Divisions, including the 
Fire Chief, shall be at the minimum level of ninety-one at least seven days prior to the start of the 
spring and fall academies, and shall not fall below the minimum staffing level until another 
Member separates from service. 
 
C. 
Each new firefighter shall satisfactorily complete an acceptable fire academy 
program or orientation held by either the Department or the Front Range Fire Consortium prior 
to entering the ranks of the Department.  Upon completion of the fire academy, the new Member 
shall be reviewed on a regular basis as set forth in a defined probationary period, approved by the 
Department.  During the probationary period, a Member may be terminated at any time without 
cause. 
 
When shift staffing is reduced from vacancies (retirement, separation, or transfer to 
days), the use of E.O.D. time may be restricted to ensure proper shift minimum levels. 
 
D. 
The use of E.O.D. compensatory time is restricted for Platoon Personnel when a 
Member is transferred to a forty-hour week duty assignment or reassigned by the Fire Chief. 
 
 
 
 

25 
 
Company Strength 
 
For the safety of the Members and the citizens of Cheyenne, and the effective utilization 
of fire equipment, a minimum standard of three full-time, professional firefighters (one officer, 
one engineer, and one firefighter) shall be assigned to each pumper, without dual assignment, 
and a minimum of four full-time, professional firefighters (one officer, two engineers, and one 
firefighter) shall be assigned to each ladder company in service, without dual assignment, for a 
minimum staffing of twenty firefighters. 
 
All firefighting equipment dispatched to all structural fires shall consist of a minimum of 
sixteen members without an aerial ladder truck and seventeen members with an aerial ladder 
truck. Members on the first alarm, and any alarm thereafter shall be answered at the discretion of 
the Incident Commander. The Incident Commander shall have full authority to request additional 
resources, make appropriate assignments and comply with the 2 in-2 out standard.  
 
In a Mutual Aid request for assistance, the City need only respond with the requested 
equipment.  The on-the-scene Officer in Charge of such equipment may, for safety reasons, 
request additional equipment through the Authority Having Jurisdiction Incident Commander 
before involving the crew at the scene. 
 

26 
 
SECTION 16.  RESERVE MANPOWER 
 
In the event of an emergency that in the judgment of the Incident Commander 
necessitates additional manpower, the Incident Commander may call in off-duty personnel of the 
Department.  The Incident Commander may request mutual aid assistance when justified by the 
circumstances. 
 

27 
 
SECTION 17.  HOURS OF WORK 
 
The average work week for Platoon Personnel shall be fifty-three hours computed on a 
twenty-seven-day basis.  The three-shift system shall be maintained with twenty-four-hour tours 
of duty, and the existing schedule shall be continued, which will include scheduled work time 
from the hours of 0800 to 1700 with one hour off for lunch and one hour for physical fitness 
training. 
 
Beginning January 1, 2020, the employer shall begin the work shifts per work period as 
follows: the stated work period for purposes of complying with the Fair Labor Standards Act 
shall be twenty-seven days.  Each Member shall be assigned to work shifts that average 204 
hours of work per period.  To accomplish this, the Member shall be provided every 18th shift off 
unpaid (without reduction in annual salary) as a Kelly Day.  Upon implementation, a Kelly Day 
slot shall be chosen by seniority and that slot shall remain with the member until the need for 
readjustment.  Examples of readjustment would include shift transfer, promotion, or demotion. 
 
Kelly Days are for line personnel only and are not cumulative, or pro-rated.  There shall 
not be two Lieutenants off at the same time, two Engineers off at the same time, or two 
Paramedics off at the same time, occupying a Kelly Day slot.  If a Member is moved to another 
shift, the Division Chief of Operations shall assign the Member to a Kelly Day slot following the 
guidelines listed above. 
 
Day Personnel will be primarily scheduled to work four 10-hour days (40-hour work 
week) as defined in the definitions.  Shifts may begin at 0700 hours and may end at 1800 hours. 
This time may be adjusted to meet the needs of the Fire Department or the Member with the 
approval of the Fire Chief.  The schedule may have one week day, and shall have Saturday, 

28 
 
Sunday, and holidays off.  If a holiday falls on a Member’s regularly scheduled day off, the 
Member shall receive the day before the holiday or the next regularly scheduled work day off, as 
determined by the Fire Chief. 

29 
 
SECTION 18.  TRADING SHIFTS 
 
Members shall be permitted to trade shifts or parts of a shift if equal duties can be 
assumed with permission of the Battalion Chief or designee, providing there is no additional cost 
to the City.  No Member shall be permitted to compensate another Member for a shift worked, 
either in money, or in any other form of remuneration. 
 

30 
 
SECTION 19.  INCLEMENT WEATHER CONDITIONS 
 
It is the responsibility of each Member of the Department to make an honest effort to 
report to work as scheduled.  During periods of severe inclement weather, Members are 
classified as essential personnel. 
 
If a Member cannot report for duty as scheduled without an unreasonable risk to his or 
her safety, the Member shall immediately notify an Officer in Charge at Headquarters by the 
hours specified in the rules and regulations to report the Member’s status.  The following will 
apply to: 
Shift Personnel: 
 
The Officer in Charge shall notify the Member who has to be relieved of duty and place 
that Member on holdover until properly relieved. 
 
The affected Member shall be: 
1. 
Granted two hours of administrative leave without penalty to report to 
duty.  If a Member cannot arrive safely in the two hours, the Member may request from 
the Fire Chief or Division Chief of Operation an additional two hours of administrative 
leave without penalty (maximum of four hours administrative leave). 
2. 
If the Member is still unable to safely report to duty by noon, he or she 
may obtain an emergency time swap for the period needed to report to duty or utilize 
accrued vacation or E.O.D. time in hour increments until he or she is able to report to 
duty. 
 
 

31 
 
Day Personnel: 
 
Members who work a forty-hour work week shall be required to observe the following: 
If the City remains open and a Day Personnel Member cannot safely report for 
duty, the affected Member will contact their appropriate supervisor to report the 
Member’s status.  The Member affected by inclement weather will be allowed to flex the 
needed time to safely report to duty or utilize accrued vacation or E.O.D. time on an hour 
for hour basis for time absent until he or she is able to safely report to duty. 
If City offices are closed, or a delayed start is declared by the Mayor of the City 
of Cheyenne, Day Personnel will be granted time off without penalty. 
In the event of a delayed start, Day Personnel will be required to report for duty at 
the designated time or will be allowed to flex the needed time to safely report to duty or 
utilize accrued vacation or E.O.D. time on an hour for hour basis for time absent until he 
or she is able to safely report to duty. 
The Department will not charge accrued vacation or E.O.D. compensatory time 
for time missed in the event a Day Personnel Member is directed by the Fire Chief or his 
or her designee to leave work early due to inclement weather. 
 

32 
 
SECTION 20.  HOLIDAYS 
 
The following are the recognized paid holidays for this contract period: 
 
New Year’s Day 
 
Martin Luther King, Jr., Day 
 
President’s Day 
 
Memorial Day 
 
Juneteenth National Independence Day 
 
July 4th 
 
One-half day for Cheyenne Day 
 
Labor Day 
 
Columbus Day (Shift Personnel only) 
 
Veteran’s Day 
 
Thanksgiving Day 
 
Friday after Thanksgiving Day (Day Personnel only) 
 
Christmas Day 
 
Department Members shall be granted a choice of either E.O.D. compensatory time off 
for the actual number of hours worked during the holiday or paid compensatory wages at his or 
her regular base rate for that shift on the holiday hours worked and shall not be deprived of and 
shall receive in addition to his or her regular base pay for the day. 
 
If any of the holidays designated in this Section should fall on a Saturday, Day Personnel 
shall receive the Friday before off with full pay. 
 
If any of the holidays designated in this Section should fall on Sunday, Day Personnel 

33 
 
shall receive the Monday that follows off with full pay. 
 
The Department shall refrain from scheduling activities on the holidays designated 
herein. 

34
